Katalog Berita (Halaman 43/57)

#2957. What's ahead for AI financing after Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang departs

Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang's departure hasn't produced a broad shock to the credit market, but it is putting the company's role as a financier of artificial intelligence infrastructure under scrutiny.

Huang, who stepped down Thursday amid an espionage and adultery scandal, built Nvidia into an AI powerhouse in the 33 years since he co-founded the company. Chief financial officer Colette Kress has been named interim CEO as the company searches for a permanent chief executive.

Nvidia not only supplies its graphics processing units to hyperscalers and tech giants, it also provides financing to other companies to help the industry expand. That could all change.

"Although we expect the company to fulfill all of its previously announced commitments, we believe that going forward, it may be more difficult for the company to negotiate new multi-party investments among ecosystem partners at the same scale it has done up to this point," Goldman Sachs analyst James Schneider said in a note Thursday.

The news of Huang's resignation comes after investors were already concerned about the massive amount of debt being taken on to fund AI infrastructure.

Tech bond yields have risen and spreads have widened on credit default swaps, most notably in lower-rated names. Credit default swaps are contracts in which investors pay for protection in case the borrower can't repay its debt. Wider spreads indicate investors view the bonds as riskier.

On Friday, the yield on Nvidia's 4.5% bond due in 2031 sat at 5.717%, nearly 5% higher week to date. Bond yields move inversely to prices.

Tech stocks slumped Friday with the Nasdaq 100 closing down 1.2% as markets continued to digest the Nvidia news. Yields on hyperscaler bonds ticked slightly higher on Friday, signaling the broader concern about AI debt lingers.

It's the smaller AI companies that could ultimately see an impact if Nvidia makes any changes to its AI financing. The company had $36 billion in off-balance-sheet AI cloud agreements, or backstops, for neoclouds, according to its last quarterly financial filing in July.

Neoclouds CoreWeave, Nebius and IREN are all in Nvidia's portfolio. Their shares were down sharply Thursday.

CoreWeave, which has become the poster child for AI debt fears, saw its bond yields retreat after its quarterly earnings on Tuesday eased investor concerns. The yield on its 9.75% bond due in 2031 was 14.79% on Friday after climbing to 16.7% last week. The spread on its five-year CDS also dropped more than 17% week to date, although it remained above 1,000 — which is generally considered a sign of distress.

It remains unclear how Nvidia's support for those companies may change, said CreditSights analyst Andy Li.

"We don't know what the ultimate strategy for Nvidia is going to be coming out the other side," he said.

"However, I think directionally, just given the fact that Jensen was so crucial in selling the Nvidia story and being a very aggressive deal maker, it's likely that a lot of this creative deal-making and circular financing and equity and credit support is going to moderate," he added.

CoreWeave CEO Michael Intrator told CNBC Wednesday, before Huang's departure, that Nvidia "is not one person."

"There are thousands of people over there that interface with thousands of people at my company every day. They integrate on the technology. They integrate on the physical infrastructure. They integrate on the networking," he said in an interview with "Squawk on the Street." "They have been a great partner for us for years. They will continue to be a great partner for us."

Bob Lang, founder and chief strategist at Explosive Options, doesn't see any big changes in terms of Nvidia's relevance or its ability to finance for now. As CFO, Kress was involved in the financing deals, and the search for a permanent CEO is going to take some time, he noted.

The real issue is just how much money the company will want to lend for AI financing, rather than, say using its hundreds of billions in free cash flow for other purposes, like share buybacks, capex, or acquisitions.

"Big companies like Nvidia and Meta, they're holding all the cards," he said. "When they decide that the game is over, that's it — it's going to be curtains for some of these smaller companies."

— CNBC'S Kif Leswing and Isabel O'Brien contributed reporting.

#2951. 76ers rule out Jaylen Brown (hamstring), list Joel Embiid (illness) as questionable for Friday's game vs. Pacers

The Philadelphia 76ers could be short-staffed when they take on the Indiana Pacers in NBA Cup play on Friday night.

Shooting guard Jaylen Brown has already been ruled out with a hamstring injury. Center Joel Embiid is listed as questionable due to a lingering illness that has sidelined him for Philadelphia's last two games.

Brown picked up the hamstring issue in Wednesday's NBA Cup win over the Miami Heat. He left the game late in the first half and did not return. The guard missed two games last week with a left hamstring strain, but returned to action against the Portland Trail Blazers and played 29 minutes.

Toward the end of the first half, the Philadelphia guard reached toward his left hamstring and shook his head before checking himself out of the game.

Embiid is dealing with an illness, though not much has been released about what symptoms he may be dealing with. Regardless, he has missed two consecutive games. The center was listed as questionable for Wednesday's game against the Heat, but was downgraded before the game.

With no Embiid and no Brown for a half, LeBron James and Tyrese Maxey took over and led the 76ers to a win over the Heat on Wednesday. James logged his first triple-double as a 76er with 25 points, 10 rebounds and 12 assists. Maxey added 30 points, 4 rebounds and 6 assists.

The win moved Philadelphia to 15-4. Friday's game against Indiana is the front end of a back-to-back, and a win would help the 76ers clinch their NBA Cup group.

#2950. Is a bottom in for the Nasdaq-100? Charts to watch

(See the video above for Frank's complete chart breakdown.)

Is the Nasdaq-100's recent bounce a sign that the bottom is in? We're going to look at several charts to help answer that question.

First, let's remember how we got here. In September, the NDX broke out from a bullish cup-and-handle pattern, producing a 33,200-upside target. That breakout quickly failed, however, and gave way to a bearish pattern that broke down aggressively two weeks ago.

The decline brought the index relatively close to its 26,350 downside target before the current rebound began.

Zooming out, it's telling to see where the bounce originated. Three important forms of support converged in the 25,000-26,000 area:

This confluence of support helped encourage buying after a roughly 15% decline.

For perspective, we also see what larger drawdowns of 20%, 30%, 40%, and 50% would look like … a lot worse.

The following chart tracks NDX drawdowns from 52-week highs going back to just after the financial crisis. Only a handful of declines exceeded 20%, and three produced relatively quick reversals: late 2018, the Covid lows in 2020 and April 2025.

Historically, buying into declines of approximately 15%–25% has generally paid off, even when additional downside occurred first (yellow highlights).

The chart also highlights previous declines of 10%–16% in green, putting the current pullback into perspective.

The major exception was 2022, when the NDX eventually declined nearly 36% over approximately 10 months ... a much worse outcome, which many investors are concerned could happen again now.

That raises the key question: Are we experiencing another routine correction, or the early stages of a larger bear market?

One important clue will be how far this bounce progresses.

Any rally that fails to reclaim the previous highs could establish a significant lower high. Additional lower highs would provide stronger evidence of a developing, longer downtrend.

We saw this in March 2022, when that first sharp rebound effort failed to reach the previous high before a much more severe decline followed.

The bottom line: The current bounce is encouraging, particularly given where it originated and knowing how well buying prior 10-20% drawdowns has paid off.

From here, it's all about the follow through.  We don't need to see new highs right away, but any rally from here that's met with another wave of strong selling could reignite downside momentum.

Frank Cappelleri is the founder and president of CappThesis, an independent research firm that helps active investors through time-tested chart and statistical analysis. Prior to starting CappThesis, he spent 25 years on Wall Street as an equity sales trader, technical analyst, research sales specialist and desk strategist.

The Federal Reserve Board on Friday released the results of the 2025 Survey of Consumer Finances, which provides the public and policymakers with detailed insights into the economic condition of American families.

The data provide a representative picture of what Americans own, how and how much they borrow, and how they bank. The current version of the survey has been undertaken every three years since 1989 and provides the public with an extensive time series of thorough information on the economic conditions of American families. Alongside the survey data, the Board issued its summary report entitled Changes in U.S. Family Finances from 2022 to 2025 (PDF), which examines changes in income, net worth, assets, debt, and financial vulnerability.

Key findings from the 2025 survey include:

In addition to the summary report, an interactive chartbook displaying changes in these financial characteristics over time is available here. Additional data from the survey, including extensive data examining other economic conditions of American families, is made available to researchers and the public here.

The survey is conducted for the Board by NORC, a social science research organization at the University of Chicago. Participants in the study are chosen at random from 119 geographic areas, including metropolitan areas and rural counties across the United States, using scientific sampling procedures that aim to produce a sample representative of American families.

#2943. What are Spurs fans thankful for this Thanksgiving?

The Spurs had a tough loss in Portland before the Thanksgiving break and will face arguably their biggest challenge yet when they face the West-leading Lakers after it. We are getting close to a quarter of the season being on the books, and games are starting to feel more meaningful as most teams round into shape.

As the season progresses, it gets harder to sit back and just enjoy San Antonio basketball without any concerns, but fortunately Thanksgiving offers the perfect opportunity to think about all the good times it has provided already and to reflect on how lucky fans of the Silver and Black really are.

Lee Dressie had a fantastic post about it that you should check out, but it would be interesting to see what you, the readers, are thankful for. Here's my list, as a way to get things started.

This one is self-explanatory. Wembanyama is the most tantalizing talent to hit the league in a generation, and he's a Spur. Luck got him to San Antonio, but he embraced the fanbase and seems to be happy to be in the city. He's lived up to the hype and seems like a perfect fit for the franchise, adapting to its traditions and ways while staying eager to forge his own path.

Watching Wemby is a joy for anyone who loves basketball, but it's extra special to watch him dominate while wearing Silver and Black.

The Spurs will be without Dylan Harper due to a hip injury for a while longer. Fans have not even gotten to see how Tobias Harris looks on this team, as he's been out since before preseason with a calf strain. A dirty play by Jeremiah Fears has forced De'Aaron Fox to sit out a few games. San Antonio has had its share of injuries.

Fortunately, none of those injuries have sidelined anyone for the rest of the year and, if everything goes well (knocks frantically on wood), all three players should be back relatively soon. Other contenders have not been so lucky, so it's important to be thankful for the injury luck the Spurs have had so far.

Sometimes fans are forced to root for players that, while talented, are not particularly likable. Whether because they are out of touch after becoming rich and famous or because of their personalities or bad behaviour, it can occasionally be hard to actually like the guys wearing the jersey. Luckily, that's not a problem with the current Spurs.

Sure, Wembanyama can come off as arrogant, but he also seems to have a good moral compass and is boring in a good way, putting basketball above everything. De'Aaron Fox always has a smile on his face and has adapted to any role asked of him. Harrison Barnes is the ultimate professional and a very charitable person. The young guards are hard workers who are willing to continue to improve instead of relying on talent and athleticism alone. Devin Vassell is soft-spoken but committed to winning, no matter what it takes.

Even the guys who have struggled, like Keldon Johnson and Luke Kornet, have great personalities that make them lovable. It's really hard to find someone to dislike on the roster, which makes watching the Spurs a lot more fun.

This one is important to remember, especially after rough losses. The Spurs have fallen short in four games this season and have had some close calls against inferior opponents. It's completely understandable to have some concerns about the team during the bad times, and occasionally even during the good times. The good news is, while there are some issues, none seem unsolvable.

The Spurs have a ton of talent, so they can overcome some mistakes, which is great. The three-point shooting has been so bad that it's bound to improve. The bench play has been poor, but it shouldn't be impossible to find some lineups that work better. Having Harris back will certainly help the second unit. And if all else fails, a trade or a signing could give the team a boost if it needs one. San Antonio is in a great place already, but things could realistically get even better.

Now let's get to the important part. What are you thankful for as a Spurs fan? Let us know in the comments.

The Federal Open Market Committee approved the following statement for release by a 10 â 2 vote:

The Committee decided to maintain the target range for the federal funds rate at 3-3/4 to 4 percent, in support of the Federal Reserve's dual mandate. The Committee is continuing its policy of maintaining ample reserves in the banking system.

Economic activity is expanding at a strong pace. While uncertainty remains elevated owing, in part, to geopolitical developments, domestic spending has been resilient. Inflation remains elevated. The accumulation of more information will help the Committee determine the policy stance appropriate to ensure a timely return of inflation to the Committee's 2 percent goal. The Committee will deliver price stability.

Voting against the monetary policy action were Lisa D. Cook and Beth M. Hammack, who preferred to raise the target range for the federal funds rate by 1/4 percentage point at this meeting.

#2937. Lakers’ Austin Reaves gets crucial injury upgrade ahead of Spurs game

Austin Reaves moved one step closer to making his return to the court on Thursday.

The Lakers star was upgraded to questionable on the team's injury report for Friday's road game against the Spurs in San Antonio.

Reaves was sidelined for the Lakers' previous five games because of a bruised left heel injury he suffered during the third quarter of their Nov. 11 road win over the Bucks in Milwaukee.

Austin Reaves (15) of the Los Angeles Lakers shoots a free throw. NBAE via Getty Images

The team deemed his status day-to-day, but Thursday was the first time he was listed as questionable on one of the team's injury reports since he's been sidelined.

The 6-foot-5 star guard was a full participant in the team's practice on Wednesday.

"It felt good to play basketball again," Reaves said in his first public comments since suffering the injury. "Wind was a little bad, but kind of to be expected being out two weeks. But felt good."

Austin Reaves has been sidelined for five games because of his heel injury. NBAE via Getty Images

He wasn't sure what happened on the play against the Bucks to cause the injury.

"I had no idea," he said. "Tried to play through it, and then I drove baseline, planted on it, and was like, I can't do this. So walked to the back and was in some pretty bad pain. I didn't think I broke my foot, but it sure felt like I broke my foot."

The Lakers went 4-1 after Reaves suffered the injury, but the team clearly missed his offensive production.

They had a 112.9 offensive rating (tied for No. 18) in the five games Reaves missed compared to the 121.6 (No. 1) offensive rating in their first 13 games.

"Who would have thought that not having an elite offensive player, an All-Star level player would hurt your offense," coach JJ Redick quipped. "That's crazy."

Los Angeles Lakers' Austin Reaves suffered his heel injury against the Bucks. AP Photo/Aaron Gash

Reaves is averaging 24.7 points (51.3% shooting from the field, 40.9% on 3-pointers), 4.7 assists and 3.3 rebounds on the season.

"One of those things that just, it sucks," Reaves said. "I was rolling, playing well. We were rolling as a team, wanted to continue to be out there and play games. Hate missing games. But it's one of those things, like I said, there wasn't much that we could really do. But we took care of business."

#2930. Observations after Sixers' 5-game win streak ends with blowout loss to Blazers

Observations after Sixers' 5-game win streak ends with blowout loss to Blazers originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers' five-game winning streak was snapped Monday night by the Trail Blazers.

With a 128-111 defeat at Xfinity Mobile Arena, the Sixers dropped their first game of the year against Western Conference opposition and fell to 14-4. Portland improved to 11-6.

LeBron James had a season-high 23 points and six rebounds. Tyrese Maxey scored 22 points and Jaylen Brown added 20.

Deni Avdija led the Blazers with 31 points and seven assists. Donovan Clingan posted 15 points and 18 rebounds.

The Sixers were missing Joel Embiid (illness). Ja Morant (left hamstring strain) and Shaedon Sharpe (right knee meniscus surgery) were among Portland's sidelined players.

Next on the Sixers' schedule is a Wednesday night game vs. the Heat. Here are observations on their blowout loss to the Blazers:

Jabari Walker, listed at 6-foot-7, defied conventional wisdom by winning the opening tip-off against the 7-2 Cllingan. Maxey then canned a three-pointer.

Brown scored the Sixers' next four points with a pair of free throws and a short jumper. VJ Edgecombe's layup gave the team a 9-2 lead. Brown and Dean Wade both returned Monday after sitting out the last two games with left hamstring injuries.

Avdija got hot in the first quarter. He scored 11 quick points, made his first four field goals and helped the Blazers avoid a serious early deficit.

The Sixers took a 32-30 edge with 9.3 seconds left in the first when Walker jammed in a put-back dunk. The former Portland second-round draft pick played 17 minutes in his second start of the season and continued to look deserving of a rotation spot. He posted 10 points and five rebounds.

Adem Bona was available again following an eight-game absence with a cervical spine sprain. However, Dominick Barlow checked in first to back up Walker.

Barlow had an outstanding start to his night. He scored a clever lefty push shot over Clingan and threw down a dunk in the first quarter. Barlow also made a highlight defensive play in his opening stint, defending Avdija in isolation and rejecting his layup attempt.

Bona then subbed in for Barlow with 3:23 left in the first quarter. He had a positive first possession, grabbing an offensive rebound and driving in for a dunk.

Wade later played at center, too. Barlow logged 16 minutes and had six points. Bona recorded four points and two boards in five minutes. Sixers head coach Nick Nurse ended up using a 10-man rotation and did not play rookie guard Labaron Philon Jr. until fourth-quarter garbage time.

Overall, the Sixers' defense remained considerably worse without Embiid. The team also conceded 15 offensive rebounds, including five to Clingan.

"I think your rim protection and ability to do that with some size is always a focal point of being really good defensively," Nurse said. "And then you've got to do it a little bit by committee (with Embiid out). I thought, for the most part, I was looking at possessions where I liked our help into the paint. They were doing a good job of zinging it out.

"I didn't particularly like our (defense) back out. … There were a couple of times they missed those and still got rebounds. We were compacted in the lane with everybody because we didn't get back out, and then still giving up an offensive rebound as well. That's just playing through the possession, a little bit of energy, all that kind of stuff. … We just didn't do enough pieces well."

Brown delivered tons of efficient offense for the Sixers in the first half and sure didn't appear rusty. His layup late in the second quarter tied the game at 58-all. The five-time All-Star scored 19 first-half points on 8-for-10 shooting.

Avdija exceeded Brown's production, hitting heat-check threes, leaners through contact and just about everything he put up regardless of the defender. He tallied 25 points in the first half and shot 9 for 12 from the field.

James reached 16 points when he capped a high-scoring half by nailing a corner three. The contest was even at 69 apiece heading into halftime.

Monday's game was James' first of the season with three or more made three-pointers. The four-time MVP went 4 for 5 beyond the arc. He'd gone 10 for 46 (21.7 percent) over his first 13 games and was due for a good long-range night.

James was whistled for his third foul early in the third quarter. Brown picked up his fourth with 10:15 to go in the period, forcing him to sit for an extended stretch.

"I wasn't too happy about it. … I think that affected the game," Brown said. "It also affected my rhythm a little bit in the second half. I had to sit (almost) the whole third quarter. Coming back from injury, coming back from a hamstring thing, my body didn't really feel the same after that."

Brown noted postgame that he had "a little bit of soreness and stuff like that."

"I think everything will check out," he said. "The medical staff is on top of everything. Those hamstrings are tricky, so you want to make sure you play it the right way, be safe. I also don't want to miss a lot of games, so I might be out there, but I've also got to be smart. I think I played it the right way today."

Avdija stopped lighting up the Sixers in the third period, but the team still had another unimpressive defensive quarter. Portland posted 32 points in the third and built its lead to double figures in the opening minute of the fourth.

The game devolved into a blowout. An Avdija dunk put the Blazers' advantage at 22 points with 5:15 on the clock and the Sixers didn't have a miracle comeback in store.

Even with all their wins, there's no question the Sixers have been trending downward on defense over the past couple of weeks, allowing regular dribble penetration and plenty of comfortable threes.

They'll aim to raise their level on that side of the floor and expect Embiid's presence to be a major help once he's back in action.

"I think it's just mindset," Walker said. "We're capable when we want to. You need games like this every now and then just to remind you that it's not as easy as it feels sometimes. It takes effort every game.

"I think we can learn a lot from this. It's just part of the growing pains, but I'm confident we'll get back to that (early-season) level of defense. We have what it takes."

#2926. A trading strategy to ride out the Nvidia uncertainty and generate income in the process

Jensen Huang's abrupt resignation has sent shockwaves through the tech sector, dragging Nvidia's (NVDA) stock down roughly 30% since Oct. 6. The market despises uncertainty, and losing a visionary founder, particularly one without a well-telegraphed succession plan, makes it worse.

However, for opportunistic investors, this panic has made the stock cheaper (obviously) while options have become significantly more expensive. Trading in the $160s, Nvidia's valuation has taken a big haircut, yet its underlying business fundamentals remain largely intact. While Huang was the architect of Nvidia's AI dominance, the company's moat — superior GPUs, the CUDA software ecosystem, and networking expertise — does not walk out the door with him. The company's structural advantage in the global artificial intelligence rollout persists.

Buying good companies at a discount is investing 101, but the current market dynamics may do something else for those who use options.  Huang's departure guarantees that Nvidia will be operating under a cloud of transition for the foreseeable future. Executing a permanent CEO search while navigating global regulatory scrutiny takes time. Consequently, Nvidia's implied volatility (IV)—the market's pricing of expected future price swings — has spiked to highs not seen since the tariff tantrum in the first half of 2025, and this elevated IV is highly likely to persist for several months as the leadership drama slowly unfolds.

This prolonged period of high implied volatility can be a goldmine for option sellers, specifically those executing options overlays such as covered call strategies. When you buy NVDA shares at these depressed levels and simultaneously sell out-of-the-money call options against your position, the premiums you collect are directly tied to IV. Because fear and transition-related uncertainty have dramatically inflated these option prices, the income generated from selling covered calls is now significantly higher than usual.

This lowers the risk profile of holding the stock considerably over time. Consistently collecting these inflated premiums systematically lowers your overall cost basis in the underlying shares. If the stock trades sideways or recovers slowly during the CEO search, you are generating an outsized cash yield on a stagnant asset. If the stock experiences further downside turbulence, the heavy premium income acts as a substantial downside buffer, meaningfully reducing the strategy's overall risk over time.

By deploying a persistent covered call strategy, investors can leverage the exact fear driving the stock down to generate robust, risk-reducing income while maintaining exposure to the undeniable future of AI.

#2923. LeBron Log No. 6: A Rocky night, a shooting lesson and a fight over ‘Bron'

LeBron Log No. 6: A Rocky night, a shooting lesson and a fight over 'Bron' originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

It was another eventful week for LeBron James.

That's why we started the LeBron Log in the first place.

The King spent his latest week taking his teammates to the movies, fighting over his name in federal court and producing his best three-point shooting performance as a Sixer days after recording a podcast about the craft.

This week also marks 20 years since James faced the Sixers alongside Allen Iverson.

James found an appropriate choice for the Sixers' latest team outing.

He organized a private screening of I Play Rocky on Friday night for players, their families and members of the basketball operations staff. The film follows Sylvester Stallone's fight to get the original Rocky made while insisting that he play the title character.

"I mean, it's Rocky. We in Philly," James said. "I heard it was a great movie, and we had a night off. I've got a few connections, and I was able to bring that to my teammates and this organization for us to watch it. I thought it was a great movie. Unbelievably cast. Everyone looked like exactly who they were portraying. That is Hollywood for you. It was pretty cool."

The Sixers shared photos of James alongside Jaylen Brown, Kentavious Caldwell-Pope, Labaron Philon Jr., Jabari Walker, Tacko Fall and Adem Bona.

"Movie night, courtesy of LeBron," the team wrote.

The nationwide release of I Play Rocky came less than two weeks before Philadelphia celebrates the 50th anniversary of the original film on Dec. 3.

Nick Nurse could not attend because of a previous commitment, but he received positive reviews from his players.

"They went, they said it was very good, they loved it," Nurse said. "The big takeaway was how much Anthony actually looked like Rocky. Or Sylvester Stallone. One in the same."

James' first season in Philadelphia now includes a private Rocky screening. A trip up the Art Museum steps remains available for a future edition.

James and Steve Nash devoted the latest episode of their Mind the Game podcast to the art of shooting.

The two examined shooting form, confidence, repetitions, free throws and some of their favorite jumpers. James also revealed one detail that can affect what he does after catching the ball.

"I'm a guy who don't like shooting against the laces," James said. "So sometimes I'll come up on a quick stop and I'm about to raise and I can feel the laces not fixed. I'll turn that into a pump fake."

The episode was released Nov. 17, while James was experiencing an extended struggle from three-point range. It did not provide an instant fix.

James went 1 for 5 from three against Orlando and missed his lone attempt against Milwaukee. He entered Monday night's meeting with Portland shooting 21.7 percent from deep through his first 13 games.

James made all three of his first-half attempts from three. He ended the second quarter by pump-faking, stepping to his left and beating the buzzer with a corner three that tied the game at 69.

Nobody could see where the laces sat on the catch. The pump fake was clear.

James finished 4 for 5 from three and scored a season-high 23 points on 8-for-13 shooting. It was his third consecutive game with at least 20 points, following 22 against Orlando and 21 against Milwaukee.

The Sixers lost to Portland, 128-111, but James produced his best long-range performance since joining them.

Six days after discussing the art of shooting, he provided a brief demonstration.

James is also attempting to protect one of his most recognizable nicknames.

James and LBJ Trademarks LLC filed a federal lawsuit on Nov. 17 against PlayVision Inc. and its founders over a sports-betting research app initially called "Bron."

The complaint alleges that the company used an AI-generated version of James in an advertisement without his permission. The figure wore a No. 23 jersey with "Bron" across the back and resembled James through its voice, build and tattoos.

It also scrolled through wagers involving NBA players.

The lawsuit alleges that the advertisement falsely created the impression that James endorsed the app and suggested that he considered betting on NBA games. The complaint said the video received roughly 1 million views before it was removed.

"The integrity of NBA competition, and the public's confidence that players are not betting on the games they play, is of paramount importance to the league, its players and its fans," the complaint said. "Any suggestion that an active NBA player is betting on NBA games is therefore far from a trivial matter, and it should not be used for clicks and views."

The app offered odds comparisons, betting trends and tracking tools but did not directly accept wagers, according to its developer.

PlayVision renamed it "Evie" on Nov. 18, one day after James filed the lawsuit. James is seeking an injunction and unspecified damages. The case remains active in the Southern District of New York.

CLEVELAND – NOVEMBER 25: LeBron James #23 of the Cleveland Cavaliers looks to pass off when double teamed by the Philadelphia 76ers November 25, 2006 at The Quicken Loans Arena in Cleveland, Ohio. Copyright 2006 NBAE (Photo by David Liam Kyle/NBAE via Getty Images)

James spent Thanksgiving weekend in 2006 on the other side of the Sixers-Cavaliers matchup.

On Nov. 25, 2006, a 21-year-old James recorded 25 points, 11 assists, four rebounds and one block in Cleveland's 108-95 win over Philadelphia.

Donning the Cavs' old-school orange threads, he played nearly 41 minutes.

Iverson led the Sixers with 31 points. Andre Iguodala added 17 points and eight rebounds, while Kyle Korver made three three-pointers off the bench.

James recorded 11 assists that night. Nearly 20 years later, another 11-assist performance against Indiana moved him past Jason Kidd for third place in NBA history.

The 2006 meeting also came near the end of Iverson's original run with Philadelphia. The Sixers traded him to Denver less than a month later.

James was 21. Iverson was nearing his departure.

Twenty years later, James is wearing a Sixers uniform and Iverson's No. 3 hangs above his home floor.

#2921. Federal Reserve Board announces pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, and changes to streamline and adjust their check services

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 1:30 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Thursday announced pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, such as the clearing of checks, automated clearing house (ACH) transactions, instant payments, and wholesale payment and settlement services.

By law, the Federal Reserve must establish fees to recover the costs, including imputed costs, of providing payment services over the long run. The Federal Reserve expects to recover 101.5 percent of actual and imputed expenses in 2027, including the return on equity that would have been earned if a private-sector firm provided the services. Overall, price changes for 2027 will result in an estimated 1.4 percent average price increase for established, mature services.

In addition, the Federal Reserve Banks announced changes to streamline and adjust their check services. As described in the request for information on check services issued last year, the volume of checks processed by the Federal Reserve Banks is declining, but checks remain an important payment instrument for many consumers and businesses. The changes were informed by public feedback and ensure that the Federal Reserve Banks continue to provide check services efficiently. The adjustments include optimizing check processing windows during low-volume periods, increasing fees that banks and credit unions pay to process checks, and other operational updates. These changes do not impact consumers' and businesses' ability to pay with or receive checks and only impact how banks and credit unions process checks with Federal Reserve Banks.

The entire 2027 fee schedule for all of the Federal Reserve's payment services is published on FRBservices.org®. Additional information on the Federal Reserve's payments services, including service usage statistics, is available on the Board's website.

#2919. Agencies extend comment period for proposed third-party risk management guidance

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 4:15 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation, the Federal Reserve Board, the National Credit Union Administration, and the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency today announced that they will extend, until December 16, the comment period for proposed guidance on managing risks associated with third-party relationships. Comments on the proposal were originally due November 16.

The proposed guidance reflects the agencies' supervisory experience and lessons learned from examining financial institutions' third-party risk management practices. It is intended to help banks and credit unions tailor their risk management practices to the risks associated with individual third-party relationships. The proposed guidance takes a principles-based approach and, like all supervisory guidance, is non-binding.

When finalized, the federal bank regulatory agencies plan to rescind existing third-party risk management guidance and replace it with the finalized guidance to promote consistency and prudent innovation in the banking industry. NCUA also plans to rescind its existing third-party risk management guidance and will conduct its own reviews to update related guidance as needed.

Also today, the Federal Reserve Board separately announced that it will extend, until December 16, the comment period on a proposed third-party risk management guide specifically for Federal Reserve-supervised traditional community banks.

For release at 10:00 a.m. EST Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Friday released results from a survey of senior financial officers at banks about their strategies and practices for managing reserve balances. The Senior Financial Officer Survey is used by the Board to obtain information about banks' reserve balance management strategies and practices, their expectations around the size of their balance sheets and deposit balances their activity in money markets, and their views on other matters, such as operations on weekends and holidays.

The survey was conducted in collaboration with the Federal Reserve Bank of New York between September 18, 2026, and September 28, 2026, and includes responses from banks that held around three-fourths of total banking system reserve balances at the time of the survey.

#2906. Important Jusuf Nurkic Injury Update

The Utah Jazz have had a dissapointing start to the season, but it looks like things might be changing for the better. According to the Utah Jazz, Jusuf Nurkic has been upgraded to questionable.

This is great news for the Utah Jazz because different elements of Nurkic's game should make life easier for the young core. Nurkic is a great screener, and the Jazz desperately need one. This season, the Jazz have the 6th-worst PPP (points per possession) at 0.84 when scoring off screens. They also have the 7th worst field goal percentage off screens at 35.9%. It's also worth noting that the Jazz are running the 8th-most off-screen trackable plays per game. That means the Jazz are running a lot of a play that hasn't worked well. Insert Nurkic and you should see this get better.

It doesn't stop there. Utah runs the fourth most dribble handoffs in the league while scoring the fourth-least PPP (Points Per Possession).

What does this mean? The Jazz are highly reliant on screens and dribble handoffs, and it has not gone well. Jusuf Nurkic is an ELITE screener. In 2025-26, can you guess who was the #1 player in the league in screen assists per game? You guessed it, Jusuf Nurkic.

We'll see if Nurkic comes back healthy and if he's moving well, but if he is, it's no understatement to say that this could turn the season around for Utah, considering the style of offense they run.

Now, it's just questionable and not probable, but for Utah to change the status is a good sign he will play.

#2904. Federal Reserve announces College Fed Challenge winners

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 4:15 p.m. EST Share

The University of California, Los Angeles won the 23rd Annual National College Fed Challenge.

The University of California, Los Angeles (UCLA) won the 23rd annual national College Fed Challenge on Friday, a team competition that encourages undergraduate students to learn about the U.S. economy, monetary policymaking, and the role of the Federal Reserve System. Teams analyze economic and financial conditions and formulate a monetary policy recommendation, modeling the Federal Open Market Committee. The team, from Los Angeles, California, represented the San Francisco District and included Aarushi Maheshwari, Annika Chan, Atul Prasanth, Caitlyn Cox, Finn Karish, Eli Brooks (alternate), Kennedy Yang (alternate), Manasi Vartak (alternate), Ronojoy Borpujari (alternate), and Sahil Sudani (alternate). The team's advisers were Chris Surro and Ali Haider Ismail.

"I am very happy to congratulate the students who competed in this year's College Fed Challenge, especially the winners and finalists," said Federal Reserve Chairman Kevin Warsh. "They took on the same big questions we policymakers face. I'm impressed by their talent, their rigor, and their eagerness to think hard about monetary policy and the economy."

This year's College Fed Challenge included 141 schools from 34 states across the nation, the most in the competition's history. Teams submitted video presentations or participated in local competitions last month. Twenty-one semi-finalist teams then participated in question-and-answer sessions held earlier this month, and seven finalists were invited to the Federal Reserve Board for the national finals. The other national finalists were the University of Chicago in second place and the University of Maryland, College Park in third place. Teams with honorable mentions include Cleveland State University, Harvard College, Pennsylvania State University, and St. Lawrence University.

Teams were evaluated on their economic analysis, responses to the judges' questions, teamwork, and presentations.

For media inquiries, please email [email protected] or call 202-452-2955.

#2899. Berkshire breaks out to record high with fourth-quarter rally reaching 19%

(This is the Warren Buffett Watch newsletter, news and analysis on all things Warren Buffett and Berkshire Hathaway. You can sign up here to receive it every Friday evening in your inbox.)

Berkshire Hathaway's red-hot shares are ending a strong week with four consecutive fresh record high closes.

Since the rally began with the start of the fourth quarter on October 1, the Class A shares have closed at record highs 11 times as they soared 19.1%.

The much more widely held B shares closed at new highs just 10 times but are also up 19.1% from their near-term low.

The gains are in sharp contrast to the S&P 500's 1.2% dip as investors appear to be seeing Berkshire as a relatively safe haven amid worries about AI stocks and spending.

Both Berkshire classes were up 3.9% on the week as the S&P advanced by 2.5%, its best week since early August.

Berkshire's B shares are now 7.5 percentage points ahead of the benchmark for the year so far.

After erasing their post-Buffett CEO announcement losses three weeks ago, Berkshire shares are now up almost 10% since last year's shareholders meeting.

They've gained around 29% since last August's lows and are up around 18% year-to-date.

Berkshire Hathaway is due to disclose its quarterly equity portfolio snapshot on Monday when it files its Q3 13F with the SEC.

We won't know for sure until then, but numbers in the company's third quarter 10-Q, released last Saturday, indicate Berkshire continued to reduce its Bank of America stake.

It would be the ninth consecutive quarter of selling.

As of June 30, Berkshire reported holding 483.4 million BAC shares valued now at $24.7 billion, it's fifth largest equity position.

On June 30, 2024, before the selling began, Berkshire owned just over 1 billion shares valued at $41.1 billion.

Since then, the number of shares in the portfolio has decreased by 53.2%, not including any Q3 sales, while the stock price has increased by 28.3%.

If there had been no reductions, the position would be worth $52.7 billion.

The belief Berkshire sold more BAC shares in the third quarter is based on a $372 million reduction in the cost basis for equities categorized by the company as "banks, insurance, and finance."

The 10-Q also reports $2.2 billion of pretax gains from equity sales.

Barron's notes Bank of America and American Express are the only financial stocks in the portfolio that have a cost basis low enough to generate a gain like that. Amex is one of the four stocks new CEO Greg Abel indicated would be held for "decades" to come in his February letter to shareholders.

In any case, Berkshire's almost 23% stake in Amex would have required it to quickly disclose any sales.

Based on the numbers in the 10-Q, the warrants Berkshire received as part of a 2011 $5 billion investment in the bank that it used in 2017 to buy 700 million shares at $7.14 each, and BAC's average stock price in the quarter, Barron's estimates 30 to 40 million shares may have been sold.

That would be roughly in line with the 30.2 million shares sold in the second quarter.

The case isn't as good, but Barron's thinks Berkshire may have bought more Alphabet shares in the period. That's based on a $2.3 billion increase in the cost basis for the category that includes Google's parent.

Overall, it looks like it will be a relatively quiet quarter with $2.4 billion in equity purchases and $2.8 billion in sales.

In the first quarter, Berkshire bought $15.9 billion and sold $24.1 billion of equities. In the second quarter, it purchased $23.5 billion and sold $3.7 billion.

All those numbers exclude the company's buybacks of its own stock.

In a term sheet filed early Friday, Berkshire Hathaway says it is selling more than 182 billion yen ($1.1 billion) worth of bonds with maturities between 2029 and 2056 and annual interest rates between 2.6% and 4.6%.

Bloomberg points out the rates, compared to benchmarks, are higher than its previous sale this year.

It quotes a fund manager as saying market sentiment "has become more sensitive," forcing issuers to make their offerings more attractive.

In April, Berkshire sold 272 billion yen ($1.7 billion) of bonds.

Berkshire uses the proceeds from the yen-denominated bonds to protect its ongoing investments in Japanese stocks from currency fluctuations.

Warren Buffett recommends Benjamin Graham's book, "The Intelligent Investor," and outlines what he considers Graham's three most important ideas.

WARREN BUFFETT: On investment books, if you're asking about that, I would recommend the first two books that Phil Fisher wrote back around 1960, "Common Sense [Stocks] and Uncommon Profits" and the second one ["Paths to Wealth Through Common Stocks"]. They're very good books.

You know, I obviously recommend, first and foremost, [Benjamin Graham's] "The Intelligent Investor," with chapters eight and 20 are the ones that you really should read.

Two of the — well, all of the important ideas in investing, really, are in that book, because there's only about three ideas. And those — two of them are emphasized in those two chapters...

One is that — to think of yourself — to think of investing as owning a business and not buying something that wiggles around in price.

And the second one is your attitude, which ties in with that, the attitude toward the market, that's covered in chapter eight. And if you have the proper attitude toward market movements, it's an enormous help in securities.

And the final chapter is on the margin of safety, which means, don't try and drive a 9,800-pound truck over a bridge that says it's, you know, "Capacity: 10,000 pounds." But go down the road a little bit and find one that says, "Capacity: 15,000 pounds."

Berkshire market capitalization: $1,259,897,054,755

Berkshire Cash as of Sept 30: $377.4 billion (Up 3.3% from June 30)

Excluding Rail Cash and Subtracting T-Bills Payable: $352.3 billion (Down 1.9% from June 30)

Berkshire repurchased $9.6 billion of its shares in Q3 2026.

Berkshire's top holdings of disclosed publicly traded stocks in the U.S. and Japan, by market value, based on the latest closing prices.

Holdings are as of June 30, 2026, as reported in Berkshire Hathaway's 13F filing on August 14, 2026, except for:

The full list of holdings and current market values is available from CNBC.com's Berkshire Hathaway Portfolio Tracker.

#2897. Mavericks vs Suns Preview: Closing out NBA Cup Group Play

The Dallas Mavericks (11-7) head out on the road Friday for their final game in Group play, with a matchup against the Phoenix Suns (9-8). Dallas has already clinched West Group A, going undefeated so far. However, as they're a team with bigger aspirations past the NBA Cup, they'll look to continue playing frenetic defense against a Suns team they've not played yet this season. Dallas is the winner of four straight, while the Suns roll into this one following a streak-snapping loss to the Houston Rockets on Wednesday.

Here are three things to consider prior to tip-off:

The Suns started the year 1-6, and before losing to Houston, they were winners of four straight to push them over .500. The staunch Houston defense paired with a rough shooting night (just 5 of 32 from three) resulted in a maddening loss to end their run. Booker in particular will be looking to score with more efficiency after shooting just 6 of 18 from the field (2 of 9 from distance). The Suns are a slightly above-average three-point shooting team (36%), so Dallas will need to chase them off the line.

The Suns are BAD on the glass, last in the league in terms of rebounds per game. The Mavericks are middling, but that might be more due to their defensive activity (think rotations, guys flying around) than the team actually being bad at rebounding. Dallas needs to control the defensive glass in this one and they might be able to run away with the score. The Mavericks have been scoring well in transition as of late, so leaning on the defense and ending Phoenix possessions should be a key to victory.

Through 15 games, the superstar guard is shooting 44.4% from the field. Were that number to hold through the season, it would be his lowest percentage of his career. Over the last 10 games, he's shooting 42% from the floor and 35% from beyond the arc. During this four game win streak, Irving's connecting on just 37% of his attempts. This is a long way of saying that Dallas is winning without Irving playing incredibly efficient basketball and be on the lookout for a breakout game where he can't miss. Maybe it will be against the Suns!

#2896. Agencies announce dollar thresholds for smaller loan exemption from appraisal requirements for higher-priced mortgage loans

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 3:00 p.m. EST Share

The Consumer Financial Protection Bureau, the Federal Reserve Board, and the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency today announced that the 2027 threshold for higher-priced mortgage loans that are subject to special appraisal requirements will increase from $34,200 to $35,500.

The threshold amount will be effective January 1, 2027, and is based on the 3.9 percent annual percentage increase in the Consumer Price Index for Urban Wage Earners and Clerical Workers, known as CPI-W, as of June 1, 2026.

The Dodd-Frank Act added special appraisal requirements for higher-priced mortgage loans to the Truth in Lending Act, including that creditors obtain a written appraisal based on a physical visit to the interior of the home before making a higher-priced mortgage loan. The rules implementing these requirements contain an exemption for loans at or below a threshold amount that is adjusted annually to reflect CPI-W increases.

Federal Register notice: Appraisals for Higher-Priced Mortgage Loans Exemption Threshold (PDF)

#2892. Agencies announce dollar thresholds for applicability of truth in lending and consumer leasing rules for consumer credit and lease transactions

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 3:00 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Reserve Board and the Consumer Financial Protection Bureau today announced the dollar thresholds used to determine whether certain consumer credit and lease transactions in 2027 are subject to certain protections under Regulation Z (Truth in Lending) and Regulation M (Consumer Leasing).

By law, the agencies are required to adjust the thresholds annually based on the annual percentage increase in the Consumer Price Index for Urban Wage Earners and Clerical Workers, known as CPI-W. Transactions at or below the thresholds are subject to the protections of the regulations.

Specifically, based on the 3.9 percent annual percentage increase in the CPI-W as of June 1, 2026, Regulation Z and Regulation M generally will apply to consumer credit transactions and consumer leases of $76,300 or less in 2027. However, private education loans and loans secured by real property, such as mortgages, are subject to Regulation Z regardless of the amount of the loan.

Federal Register notice: Consumer Leasing (Regulation M) (PDF)

Federal Register notice: Truth in Lending (Regulation Z) (PDF)

#2890. Game Preview #20: Wolves vs Grizzlies Game Thread

Happy Thanksgiving, everyone. As your holiday winds down, I'm pleased to bring you a brief preview of the next Minnesota Timberwolves game, which is against the Memphis Grizzlies.

Who: Minnesota Timberwolves (14-5) at Memphis Grizzlies (14-5)

This matchup will be the final game of the group stage for the NBA Cup, and the winner will get an automatic place in the cup playoffs. The Wolves have yet to reach that stage of the tournament, so putting together a win to make it there would be yet another great chapter within the storybook of this resurgent season.

A loss would not be ideal, though it would not be the end of the world as far as the Cup is concerned. Minnesota is in a pretty good position to make it in as a wildcard team over the Denver Nuggets based upon point differential. Obviously winning any game is the best-case scenario, though the Wolves have multiple paths towards competing for the increasingly-coveted NBA Cup title.

Regardless of Cup implications, this should be yet another good matchup between two teams that have been consistently strong in the Western Conference. Minnesota took the last matchup at home 104-95 which featured strong performances from Jaden McDaniels, Ayo Dosunmu, Jonathan Kuminga, and Terrence Shannon Jr. The Wolves will need to keep up their positive momentum from all of those guys if they look to clinch the group and pass the Grizzlies in the Western Conference standings.

That said, it may still be tough sledding for an already-injured team that may be dealing with another one for the foreseeable future. Keeping that in mind, here are a couple things I'm looking for in the game tomorrow:

Watching LaMelo Ball gingerly limp off of the court against the Oklahoma City Thunder was not a welcome sight. We got the win, but everyone is hoping that his turned ankle does not turn out (pun intended) to be anything serious.

It would not be out of the realm of possibility for Ball to miss this game. However, the team that used to boast significant guard depth is looking considerably weaker if we have the quadrio of Ball, Anthony Edwards, Donte DiVincenzo, and Jaylen Clark all sidelined.

As it were, this means that Bones Hyland should get a sizable helping of playing time. I would not be shocked to see G-League stud Zyon Pullin also get a fair bit of run to ease what would otherwise be a hefty serving of minutes for Hyland and Dosunmu. We've learned this season just how important adequate point guard play is with the addition of Ball, and I do not think Chris Finch would overwork the two guards left that are capable of stuffing that position.

On the other side of the scorer's table, the Grizzlies also have their share of injuries to deal with. Rookie forward Cameron Boozer is still out with injury, and Zach Edey is a game time decision after picking up a knock in their last game against the Los Angeles Clippers. With Jerami Grant, GG Jackson, and Scottie Pippen Jr. all still on the injured list as well, Memphis has had plenty of guys step up and give the team quality minutes in their absences. They may need more help if they are looking to defeat the Wolves on Friday.

What can't Jaden McDaniels do? Nothing, apparently. The guy has been a machine as of late, averaging 26 points per game while shooting the ball well over 67% from the floor and 69% from deep across his last five games. Those are some nice numbers.

On top of that, Jaden is continuing to put the clamps on opposing teams' best players. He locked up Shai Gilgeous-Alexander at the end of the game last evening, and McDaniels never appears to tire across the course of the game. He's doing it all for the Timberwolves, and that production has been necessary in the wake of the Edwards injury.

There is a legitimate argument to be made regarding Jaden as an All Star candidate this season. That argument will be bolstered if he can continue to control games with Ball sidelined. It's not McDaniels's fault that he is in this position to carry the team on his back, though his admirable performances are earning attention league-wide.

Next man up is always the mentality, and Jaden has been doing his part in keeping this injury-addled team afloat. Adding another injury to that mix certainly changes the dynamic of the team, though McDaniels has been playing strong enough that he should be able to overcome the absence of Ball.

I've mentioned in past articles that while Ball hasn't had the most statistical significance across the last couple weeks, he is unquestionably controlling the game and making the offense easier for everyone else. With him out, someone else will have to step up to make some shots and draw the defense in their direction. That person should be Terrence Shannon Jr.

He's had a few up-and-down performances lately, and his last game against the Thunder saw him score only eight points on 2/10 shooting while turning the ball over three times. Minnesota won the game, but they will need more production out of TJ with Ball being out against a tough opponent in Memphis.

A brief positive for TJ could be conceptualized with the news of Ball's absence, as the ball will likely be in the hands of Shannon more during his minutes. Last year gave the film buffs tons of evidence about how effective TJ can be as a scorer if he is initiating the offense. If he has more opportunity to control the lineups when he is deployed, he should be able to find a rhythm quickly.

Efficient scoring performances from TJ are always welcome sights. He has already been an integral part of this team with the current injury situation, and that importance will be inflated with yet another injury to a key player that plays heavy minutes. Expect Finch to lean on Shannon early and often in this contest.

This one is going to be a nail-biter, but I can't bet against the Wolves no matter who we play against. Expect TSJ to have a strong scoring night and, ideally, a grand re-opening of the Bone Zone. I think the Wolves take this one, but not by much.

#2886. Sovereign wealth funds are acting more like private equity giants — and inheriting their risks

For decades, sovereign wealth funds were among private equity's most deep-pocketed backers, entrusting billions of dollars to buyout firms to invest on their behalf. Increasingly, they are doing the dealmaking themselves.

From originating transactions and taking controlling stakes in companies to building their own investment platforms, some of the world's largest state investors are adopting the playbook of traditional private equity giants, according to industry veterans.

"The imminent change is that the lines between LPs and GPs are becoming increasingly blurry. This could bring more fundamental changes in the long term," Diego Lopez of Global SWF told CNBC.

Limited partners, or LPs, typically commit capital to private equity funds, while general partners, or GPs, are responsible for sourcing deals and managing investments.

Sovereign funds and state investment companies are increasingly taking on the GP role, Lopez said, pointing to the establishment of their own private equity management platforms, such as Mubadala Capital and Singapore's Seviora.

The shift is also reflected in the way some of the world's largest sovereign investors are approaching dealmaking.

"They originate transactions rather than waiting for them. They build platforms rather than buying single assets. They take control positions and operate what they own," PwC said in their latest report on sovereign wealth funds.

"Whether you're a company, a general partner, or a government, you'll find that these institutions are no longer the limited partner you're raising money from; they're the principal sitting across the table."

Last year, Abu Dhabi's MGX, an investment vehicle established by state fund Mubadala in 2024, anchored the roughly $40 billion acquisition of Aligned Data Centers alongside BlackRock, Nvidia and Microsoft. A year earlier, a consortium led by Mubadala Capital acquired a 68% equity stake in alternative asset manager Fortress, which at the time managed more than $48 billion.

Some sovereign funds are striking deals at a speed typically associated with large private equity firms, according to PwC.

Singapore's GIC recorded 553 transactions between July 2023 and July 2026, Abu Dhabi's Mubadala 397 and the Abu Dhabi Investment Authority 367, according to PwC's analysis of PitchBook data, which includes transactions by subsidiaries.

The shift comes as sovereign wealth funds command unprecedented financial firepower. Their assets have surged about 59% over the past five years to $16.5 trillion, up from $10.4 trillion in 2022 and more than nine times the $1.8 trillion they managed in 2005, according to data provided to CNBC by research firm Global SWF which focuses on state-owned investors.

PwC projects that sovereign wealth fund assets to reach $22.4 trillion by 2030.

While sovereign funds' push into direct investing has been underway for years, a more challenging fundraising environment for PE firms and a resurgence in megadeals have recently accelerated the trend.

The fundraising slowdown has tilted bargaining power toward large institutional investors, allowing them to negotiate more opportunities to invest directly alongside private equity managers, said Kyle Walters of PitchBook.

A key attraction is the potential to save on fees. Co-investments typically carry little or no management fees and no carried interest, which is the share of investment profits that managers receive. Additionally, direct deals can eliminate external manager fees altogether.

"For a large SWF writing big checks, the fee savings in dollar terms can be substantial," Walters said.

Sovereign funds also enjoy structural advantages over traditional buyout firms. Unlike conventional private equity funds, which operate within predetermined lifespans, sovereign investors can hold assets for decades, the experts highlighted.

They can also deploy substantial sums from their own balance sheets without having to raise fresh capital, an advantage particularly valuable in capital-intensive sectors such as AI infrastructure and energy.

But deep pockets and patient capital alone do not guarantee success.

As sovereign wealth funds take controlling stakes in companies, they assume operational responsibilities traditionally borne by private equity managers, while potentially stretching their own capacity to source deals, conduct due diligence and manage complex assets, PwC cautioned.

"Building the required capability, the talent, the systems, and the governance is a slower process than committing the capital," PwC said. The firm also flagged concentration risks as sovereign investors increasingly pile into similar investment themes, particularly AI, data centers and power infrastructure, leaving even geographically diversified portfolios vulnerable to the same underlying risks.

Despite their growing ambitions, industry experts are skeptical that sovereign wealth funds will supplant established private equity managers.

"This new path these large LPs are paving is more of a partnership than a competition between GPs and LPs," Walters said, noting that much of the increased activity is taking the form of co-investments.

He pointed to the $55 billion take-private deal for video-game maker Electronic Arts announced last year, in which Saudi Arabia's PIF contributed significant capital, as an example of how both sides ae joining hands.

Traditional buyout firms also retain an edge in sourcing deals and managing portfolio companies.

"However, capital alone does not replace the specialist sourcing, operational expertise, and active ownership capabilities required to consistently create value, which experienced managers have built over time," said Vincent Ng, managing director and co-head of Asia at Partners Group.

Direct investing also raises concentration risks, while illiquidity could become a problem during crises when governments need access to capital, experts said.

Rather than displacing traditional buyout firms, sovereign investors' growing ambitions could change the economics of private equity, forcing managers to become more flexible in how they work with their biggest clients.

"This is likely to reshape the relationship between asset owners and managers, with more customized mandates, co-investments, and long-term partnerships," Ng said.

#2883. Melton hit 8 threes on Kings, joined an exclusive Warriors’ club

De'Anthony Melton nailed eight threes against Sacramento on Wednesday night and sent me down a rabbit hole (brought to you by Stathead) that ate up a lot more of my Thanksgiving than I planned.

Melton tied his career high with eight threes and scored 24 points in the 129-110 NBA Cup win over the Kings, and he did it with Steph sitting out with a torn oblique. He went 8-for-12 from deep, 8-for-14 overall, and never took a free throw.

With those eight treys, Melton walked into one of the most exclusive shooting clubs in franchise history. All of which got me wondering how often the Warriors lose when somebody does that. The answer is: almost never.

Since Coach Steve Kerr took over in 2014, there have been 120 regular-season performances where a Warrior hit eight or more threes, spread across 119 games because Curry and Wiggins share one, and the Warriors are 92-27 in those games. That's a .773 winning percentage, a 63-win pace, and the average scoring margin across all 119 games, wins and losses together, is almost plus-10.

Melton is the eighth different Warrior to do it under Kerr. The other seven are Curry, Klay Thompson, D'Angelo Russell, Jordan Poole, Wiggins, Brandin Podziemski and Moses Moody, and if you squint, that list doubles as a decade of Warriors history.

By the way, if I told you two Warriors once hit eight threes apiece in the same game during the Steve Kerr era would you bet on that being the Splash Brothers? Wrong, dear reader. Twas' Curry and Andrew Wiggins.

But let's find some more fun facts. During the first five seasons of the Kerr era, 2014-15 through 2018-19, the Warriors went 50-4 when somebody hit eight threes. Since then they're 42-23. A 92.6% winning percentage turned into 64.6%. Remember when Steph would hit his sixth three and you'd start figuring out how to beat the traffic out of Oracle? Nowadays a sixth three means you're hoping the Warriors can get a stop at the other end.

In the 73-9 season they went 20-1 in these games, which means somebody hit eight threes in roughly one out of every four games that year. The one loss took history to pull off. On April 1, 2016, Curry hit eight threes against Boston and the Celtics still won 109-106, ending the 54-game home winning streak, an NBA record. Even the losses from that era came with a plaque.

Those dynasty teams had the defense, depth and talent to turn a hot shooting night into an early bedtime for everybody watching on the East Coast.

Curry has 88 of the 120 performances, and Steph and Klay have a combined 111 of them, 92.5% of the entire club. The Warriors are 68-20 when Steph hits eight. Through age 34, the Warriors were 56-10 in his eight-three games. Since he turned 35, they're 12-10. Since he turned 37, they're 3-3.

He's still having these nights, and that's what gets me. In January 2025, at 36, he went 8-for-8 from three against Philadelphia, the only perfect night in the entire dataset. He dropped 31 with eight threes on the Lakers a month ago at 38 years old. The miracle nights keep coming, but they used to be a guaranteed win and now they're closer to a coin flip. The worst one to look back on is February 2024 in Atlanta, when he scored 60 with 10 threes and lost in overtime. He's lost 57-point and 50-point games too. The Warriors are 10-3 when Curry scores at least 50 points and makes at least eight threes, and they went 4-0 when Klay did it. When Steph has gone nuclear lately, he's usually been making up for everything around him.

Across his whole Warriors run under Kerr, 2014-15 through 2023-24, Klay went 19-4 in his eight-three games and 13-1 at home. He scored 60 points in 29 minutes against Indiana and sat out the fourth quarter. He hit 14 threes in 27 minutes in Chicago, which is still the franchise record, and he once went 10-for-11 from deep against the Lakers.

Not counting Steph and Klay, the record is 6-3. Two of those losses belong to D'Angelo Russell, who hit nine threes against Dallas on Dec. 28, 2019 and nine more against Indiana on Jan. 24, 2020, and lost both. The Dallas game was a 20-point loss, still the worst in the whole dataset. That's the most 2019-20 Warriors thing imaginable, and I say that with love for everyone who suffered through that season.

Poole went 1-1 and Wiggins went 2-0. Podziemski went 1-0 against the Lakers in April 2025, and Moody went 1-0 last November in New Orleans when he hit seven threes in one quarter and joined Steph and Klay as the only Warriors ever to do it.

A 6-3 record for the non-Splash Brothers isn't a big enough sample to call it a new formula, and one bench explosion against Sacramento doesn't change the math from the last few years. These days the shooting is sometimes the only thing keeping the Warriors in the game.

That's why Wednesday night felt so good. Steph was in street clothes nursing a torn oblique, somebody else got to be the reason an opposing coach was staring at the ceiling wondering what he did to deserve this.

#2881. Fast cars, big money: Why sportswear races into Formula 1 as sponsorship spending soars 75%

The race for dominance in Formula 1 is no longer confined to the racetrack, with some of the world's biggest sportswear brands, including Nike, Adidas and Puma, vying for a piece of the action.

Sportswear sponsorship spending in F1 has surged 75% over two years and topped $200 million in 2026, according to Adam Lewis, a senior analyst at research firm Ampere Analysis.

Brands are competing for access to the sport's increasingly young and affluent fan base, while looking to capitalize on its growing influence in fashion and popular culture.

Nike gave fans a sneak preview of its F1 partnership in Las Vegas this month with a limited-edition collection sold during the Grand Prix. From 2027, Nike will supply F1 staff uniforms, a role Puma has held since 2024, and sell footwear and apparel under the championship's brand.

Other sportswear majors have taken a different approach. Adidas signed with Mercedes in 2025 and added Audi for its debut season this year, while Puma still has its longstanding deal with Ferrari, covering racewear and licensing for Ferrari-branded products.

Unlike football leagues such as the English Premier League, where sportswear brands spread their budgets across numerous clubs, F1 has just 11 teams, limiting the number of apparel partnerships up for grabs.

"F1, by contrast, is a closed market with only a handful of teams," Lewis said, adding that the scarcity of deals makes brands more willing to pay higher prices as contracts come up for renewal.

What makes F1 particularly attractive, however, is its growing influence.

"For major sportswear and lifestyle brands, F1 has grown beyond motorsport, into a powerful, year-round pop-culture and storytelling platform," Joey Khong, global consumer trends manager at market research firm Mintel, told CNBC.

F1 brings together fashion, music, luxury and celebrity culture with athletic performance and technological innovation, offering brands multiple avenues to engage with consumers, Khong said.

Team apparel has also crossed over into everyday streetwear, with social media amplifying the visibility of what drivers and team members wear off the track.

Some 34% of people who engage with F1 do so through social media, while 22% engage through online content, according to Lewis.

That constant stream of content has turned team apparel into what Lewis described as a "living billboard" for sportswear brands.

Nearly a third of F1 fans are now under the age of 35, with that share rising 18% since 2023, according to Lewis. Meanwhile, 45% of F1 fans fall into the two highest-earning socioeconomic groups, compared with 36% of the general population.

For sportswear brands, F1 offers a way to build loyalty among younger, affluent consumers.

Asia-Pacific has been a key driver of F1's fan growth, helping deliver a larger audience for its commercial partners, analysts said.

Markets across the region have gained more than 25 million F1 fans since 2023, up 36%, according to Ampere Analysis. That outpaced growth in other markets by 11 percentage points and accounted for 84% of overall fan growth.

That offers sportswear brands a growing audience to engage with in a region where F1 already races in Melbourne, Suzuka, Shanghai and Singapore.

Research also points to why that engagement could resonate. In a 2026 Mintel survey, 29% of respondents in China said sporting events would prompt them to pay attention to or try related brands, rising to 37% in households earning at least 24,000 yuan ($3,600) a month.

Fans are spending more on F1 merchandise than ever before.

F1 merchandise sales have grown by almost 200% since 2017, according to Fanatics, the sport's e-commerce partner, cited in a License Global report.

Retail sales of F1-licensed consumer products also rose 44% in 2024, one of the fastest growth rates among the sports properties tracked.

The appeal may stretch beyond race-branded gear. F1 fans were 29% more likely than the average consumer to say they planned to buy sports apparel in the next year and 27% more likely to say the same about non-sports apparel, according to Ampere's consumer research.

Mintel's Khong said F1 merchandise alone is unlikely to move the needle quickly for sports apparel giants the size of Nike or Adidas.

Instead, the bigger potential payoff could come from the partnership boosting demand across the brand's everyday product lines outside F1-specific collaborations or the sport's licensed merchandise.

"There is a route to revenue growth, but being associated with the sport is only the starting point," Khong said.
