#2787. Dan Campbell on calling late timeouts: I was hoping for a "chance for rebuttal"

With 2:34 to play on Thursday, the Lions had the ball on the Chicago 39. It was first and 10. Detroit led, 35-32.

In other words, Detroit had its ninth win in hand.

But the drive ended when coach Dan Campbell decided to go for it on fourth and four from the Chicago 33, instead of attempting a 51-yard field goal that would have stretched the lead to six.

Yes, the Bears would have gotten the ball back with more than two minutes to play, if Campbell had opted to kick (and if kicker Jake Bates had made it). Yes, a touchdown (and an extra point) would have won the game. By not converting, the Bears had a chance to play for overtime or, if they could gain enough ground, a game-winning touchdown.

After the game, Campbell addressed the decision to go for it on fourth down.

“I don’t regret that, you know?” Campbell said. “I wish we would have made that. But, listen, sometimes that happens, you know?”

While that decision becomes the obvious focus of postgame scrutiny, a subsequent decision deserves some focus, too: As the Bears drove for what ultimately was the game-winning touchdown with three seconds left, Campbell called a pair of timeouts.

He called one after the Bears got a first down at the Detroit 31, with 1:12 to play. He called another after the Bears got a first down at the Detroit 21, with 52 seconds to play.

At the time, the Bears had only one timeout. Given the Thanksgiving Day debacle from 2024, when the Bears got greedy and blew their shot at a field goal that would have forced overtime (and coach Matt Eberflus got fired the next day), they undoubtedly wouldn’t have gotten cute as the final minute ticked away.

Two years later, those two timeouts allowed the Bears to run the clock all the way down to the last few seconds before taking the lead.

Asked whether he took the timeouts to give the defense a “breather” or a chance to “regroup,” Campbell said he was hoping for a “chance for rebuttal.”

The problem with that approach is that it was premised on the Bears tying the game with a field goal. The timeouts helped ensure that the Bears would have enough time to score a touchdown.

There was still plenty of risk for Chicago. The Bears ran two plays with no timeouts, risking a sack or a tackle in the field of play that would have ended the game.

The first play flirted with disaster. With 13 seconds left and the Bears at the Lions’ six, quarterback Caleb Williams threw toward running back D’Andre Swift near the goal line. The ball was tipped by linebacker Derrick Barnes. If it had gotten through to Swift, he may have caught it and been tackled short of the end zone. And the Bears most likely would have been unable to rush to the line, get in formation, snap the ball, and spike it.

Or the Swift catch could have been ruled a touchdown on the field and then overturned by replay review, with a 10-second runoff that would have ended the game. The Lions know that wrinkle all too well, thanks to an identical outcome in a 2017 game against the Falcons.

Still, the Bears scored a touchdown and won. And they had enough time to do it in part because Campbell called not one but two timeouts as the Bears were driving.

#2786. Knicks Bulletin: ‘Sometimes your mind or your body just takes a second’

If you still have leftovers in the fridge, congratulations. That means you'll be able to celebrate twice today.

First, a Knicks win over the Heat. Then, feasting on another round of reheated turkey, mac and cheese, and mashed potatoes.

Here's yesterday's post, slightly padded with a few more quotes.

On the Knicks still having room for improvement:

"I've said this before, we play so many games. You can never get too high. I guess we're 12-3 since the start, but our [performance] does still stink. We haven't arrived. We still have to embrace the little things.

"They do this (points downward), but eventually they're going to do this (points upward). When we do that, handling the challenges in front of us the right way is going to be big. You can't get too high in this league because you're that close to going the other way. Just like if you're down, it's not the end of the world; you're that close to flipping it. So you try to stay even-keeled and kind of stick with your process, especially if it's something that you believe in. And we believe in it. Eventually things are going to change or turn in the right direction."

On Jordan Clarkson being instrumental in establishing the Knicks' flexible bench rotation and wanting him to remain with the organization beyond his playing career:

"As a coach, you could want to do that, but you have to have others around you that will at least support it initially to see how it turns out. And a huge, huge, huge catalyst for that was Jordan Clarkson. Jordan Clarkson was the one, if you're talking about giving 100 percent out there to what allows me to do that, I think Jordan Clarkson gave me at least 90 percent of the opportunity to do it, because here's an accomplished veteran that could be in the rotation for almost any team out there, let alone a championship team, and I can literally play him six straight games and then sit him 10 straight games and he's gonna be the same dude every fricken' day. And he's gonna talk the same way to his teammates. Not 'can you believe what Mike Brown did?' Nah, he's gonna be the same dude, uplift the group, and then when I call his number again, he's gonna be ready to go. He's a guy that, even when he gets done playing, it's one of those things where you hope that he can just be a part of this organization in some way, shape or form until he doesn't want to be part of this organization. He's that type of person, in my opinion."

On the benefits of potentially missing the NBA Cup knockout rounds:

"It is important (to win and potentially advance), but the reality of it is, when you look at the schedule, and you see that our percentage [to advance] is not that high, and so they kind of shared with me what our schedule would look like if we don't go and if we do go. And you look at it, you're like, 'Whoa, that's a lot of days off if you don't go.' And so again, you want to compete; you want to win. But if you don't, in my opinion, when you look at the schedule… you're like, there's an opportunity if you got some guys banged up to get rest and/or to work on some things in between the games that you have if you're not involved in the Cup."

On the physical and mental toll of competing in the NBA Cup:

"I didn't really break it down to an 83rd game, but when I talk about the Cup, it adds a level of stress maybe on your mind and your body because the games are intense as you go along and you're playing more games in a short amount of time.

"I remember it was like, game, game, game, and then you win the Cup, and you celebrate it that night, and you hop on a plane and go play in Indiana the next night. And so it's great. You like the competition and the setup, but it adds a lot of stress to you physically and mentally. I'm saying this because especially with the extended season we had last year and how taxing the games were for our playoff run mentally and physically, I'm trying to be smart, so if — knock on wood — we end up advancing, I'll try to be smart about it."

On refusing to chase regular-season wins at all costs and learning from Golden State's 73-win season:

"I try not to chase wins, especially early in the season. You want to win, but I'm not going to win every game at all costs.

"I wasn't part of the staff [for the first Golden State title] [Golden State] won Steve [Kerr's] first year. Then they lost in the second year, but they set a regular season record of 73 wins, and one of the things that he used to tell me, they gassed themselves trying to get this record and come playoff time they just didn't have enough in the tank to close it. And so that always sits right here for me."

On calling Pacôme Dadiet into his office and threatening to remove him from the rotation over his lack of effort:

"I didn't think he played hard in the game before. And I actually called him into my office and showed him some film. I told him, 'As a young guy, if you don't play hard, I can't play you.'

"And I said, 'Believe me, I got a lot of guys I can go to. I'm not afraid to go to somebody else. So the least you could do is play hard.' And I showed him how he wasn't. And I thought [Wednesday], he played hard."

On leaving OG Anunoby's injury management to the medical staff:

"There are a lot of medical terms that are above my head. They tell me and I don't really pick up on the injury. If he's out, I change my minutes sheet. And then I'll just wait for them to tell me when he's back. [Wednesday], OG's individual work happened before shootaround and I didn't even know he had individual work until his player development guy told me later on. You never want a guy to hurt himself more. But I don't think about it."

On the Knicks still having plenty of room for improvement despite winning 12 of their last 15 games:

"We're 12-3 since the start, but our s–t still does stink. We haven't arrived, and we still have to embrace the little things."

On the Knicks never panicking after their 0-3 start and gradually finding their rhythm:

"I don't think we had any panic. It wasn't like we started 0 and 3 and we were getting blown out by 15 and 20 every game. It was a possession game each of those three losses and could've gone either way. So we knew that, and we knew it was just a play here or a play there that could've changed the game. We just tried to make sure we're focusing on every possession. Sometimes your mind or your body just takes a second at the beginning of the season. I feel like we're in a good space, physically and mentally, continuing to get better. Really diving into our core principles."

On Brown experimenting with an unusually deep rotation against Cleveland:

"I looked at the bench in the first quarter and realized, 'Wow, we played like 10 people in that first quarter.'"

On the Knicks' supporting cast stepping up and trusting Karl-Anthony Towns and Jalen Brunson as their leaders:

"I mean, it's big for our team. Those guys [Towns and Brunson] are going to carry us enough, so today obviously it was an all-around game. We're always going to have their backs, and they always have ours, and it just shows you what type of team we are. It could be anybody on any night, but they're going to carry us to the promised land."

On Clarkson encouraging Pacôme Dadiet and reminding his teammates to stay ready:

"JC is always big on that, telling guys to stay ready. He was in [Dadiet's] ear the whole shootaround [Wednesday morning]. Just telling everybody to stay ready because you never know when your name is called."

On endorsing Tom Thibodeau as a potential head coaching hire for struggling NBA franchises:

"I made three playoffs with Thibodeau as my coach so his track record is one of success. So of course if I was a team that wanted to get back on track, I'd look at Tom Thibodeau to be my coach."

On Thibodeau's tactical knowledge, dedication and ability to prepare his teams to win:

"It's the X's and O's and the amount of care he puts into his craft, his team. He steps into that gym every day and, you know, you can say anything you want about the way he coaches, but one thing I know is his team is prepared to win a game. So that's a testament to who he is as a coach, the preparation he puts his team through and also his ability to have his team ready for the moment."

On the Knicks' bench players embracing fluctuating minutes and not having egos:

"We care about each other. We want to win for each other. We're willing to take on different roles and we know that we've got each other's back. If Jose's got it going and my minutes are shortened, awesome. And vice versa. All of us, Deuce [McBride] included in that, I would throw him in that as well, all of us kind of feel that way. That's really cool.

"I've been saying it, I've been a broken record about it, but you don't see that — that's very rare where no ego or ulterior motives get involved. That's something special that you want to be a part of."

#2784. Friday's game gives Steelers fans a chance to chime in on recent events

Much has been said from the folks at the middle of the current clusterfudge at the confluence of three rivers. One very important constituency has yet to chime in.

Later today, Steelers fans will get a chance to make their voices heard when the home team hosts the Broncos in a Black Friday game.

One option for Steelers fans will be to not show up. And we’ve heard some anecdotal evidence indicating that some fans have decided not to go to the game.

That may be better than the alternative. Steelers fans aren’t blindly loyal. If they see something they don’t like, they’ll make it known. Loudly.

The dynamic adds extra pressure to the situation. If things start poorly, you’ll hear the boos on the Prime Video stream, as clearly and plainly as we heard Rodgers’s comments during the Thursday night Prime Video game in Cleveland.

It’s just another reason to tune in at 3:00 p.m. ET, and to watch — and listen — closely.

#2782. Patrick Mahomes on Harrison Butker: I try to have confidence in everybody, I know he's working at it

“I try to have confidence in everybody on the team. The Chiefs signed Justin Tucker to their practice squad with the hope of pushing Butker to improve. But at this point, if Tucker is going to be around, it might be time to try him in a game.

#2778. Top Wall Street analysts favor these 3 dividend stocks for strong returns

Investors often favor dividend stocks for their regular income and the promise of potential share price appreciation. But selecting the right dividend stocks from a huge pool of companies can be difficult.

Top Wall Street analysts can help shortlist attractive dividend-paying stocks, as their recommendations are backed by thorough analysis of a company's financials.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Devon Energy (DVN), an independent oil and natural gas exploration and production company, reported better-than-expected third-quarter results. The company announced a quarterly dividend of 32 cents per share for the fourth quarter, payable December 30. Based on the fixed dividends paid over the trailing 12 months, DVN yields 2.85%.

After hosting a virtual investor meeting with management, Goldman Sachs analyst Neil Mehta reiterated a buy rating on Devon, with a price target of $59. Mehta said that he is now more confident in management's outlook for delivering strong near-term capital returns as well as efforts to drive operational efficiencies and productivity gains.

Devon sees an opportunity to increase share repurchases, driven by solid free cash flow (FCF), contributions from the Eagle Ford divestiture and continued improvement in the balance sheet, Mehta said. Devon expects share buybacks worth more than $1 billion in Q4 2026, up from $414 million in the third quarter, backed by a strong pricing backdrop and improved capital efficiency.

Mehta, a 5-star analyst, said Devon expects about $6.3 billion of excess FCF in 2027, including about $3.5 billion of net proceeds from the Eagle Ford sale. That would enable the company to accelerate buybacks while progressing toward its goal of reducing gross debt to about $9 billion by early 2027. Mehta noted that under DVN's framework to return up to 70% of annual FCF, management favors share buybacks as it believes the stock is discounted compared to large-cap peers.

"Management expects continued capital efficiency gains from merger synergies, extended laterals, surfactant adoption and development of the newly acquired federal acreage," Mehta said.

Mehta ranks No. 876 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 8.1%. See Devon Energy Stock Buybacks on TipRanks.

Energy Transfer (ET), is a limited partnership that operates about 140,000 miles of pipeline and associated energy infrastructure. The company recently announced third-quarter results showing net income per common unit growing about 18% year-over-year, to 33 cents. ET's cash distribution for Q3 2026 was 34.25 cents per common unit, up more than 3% year-over-year. At an annualized distribution of $1.37 per share, Energy Transfer yields more than 7%.

TD Cowen analyst Jason Gabelman reiterated a buy rating on Energy Transfer with a price target of $25. The company delivered better-than-expected Q3 results and announced the 1.2 billion cubic feet per day (bcf/d) South Mississippi Pipeline, a 215-mile, 36-inch natural gas line linking the Perryville Hub to the Florida Gas Transmission (FGT) network, Gabelman said. The project is supported by 20-year firm transportation agreements and is expected to enter service in 2030. ET expects to ultimately own 51% of the $1.6 billion project.

The 5-star analyst said that Energy Transfer lowered its 2026 capital spending outlook to $5.5 billion (midpoint of the guidance range) from $5.75 billion due to project timing. Energy Transfer expects capex to rise to $7 billion in 2027, while Gabelman projects $5 billion in both 2028 and 2029, based on disclosed projects.

Gabelman also highlighted management's commentary about optimization initiatives contributing an incremental $800 million to 2026 earnings before interest, taxes, depreciation, and amortization, a larger benefit than in recent years. "A return to less commodity volatility should the [Middle East] war end could bring some relative downside, though this is offset by continued strong growth prospects," he said.

Gabelman ranks No. 397 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 15.4%. See Energy Transfer KPIs on TipRanks.

Oil and gas exploration and production company ConocoPhillips (COP) delivered better-than-expected Q3 performance. The company announced an 11% hike in its quarterly dividend to 93 cents per share, effective with the Q4 dividend payable on December 1. Based on the dividends paid over the trailing 12 months, COP yields about 2.6%.

Following the Q3 results, Wells Fargo analyst Sam Margolin repeated a buy rating on ConocoPhillips, citing strong potential for further dividend increases. Margolin also trimmed his price target to $185 from $189 to reflect timing effects and the WAHA forward curve, though he said that COP could still re-rate as its natural portfolio lifecycle provides stronger protection against inflationary pressures than peers. The Waha forward curve tracks future natural gas prices based on the West Texas Waha hub.

Margolin, another 5-star analyst, highlighted that ConocoPhillips' Q3 2026 adjusted earnings per share of $3.27 was well above Wells Fargo and the Street's estimates of $3.04 and $3.05, respectively. Earnings were driven by solid price realizations, partially offset by higher depletion, depreciation and amortization and purchased commodities.

Margolin also noted COP's dividend increase and said the accelerated buyback enabled by current commodity prices could further support long-term dividend growth. He sees the possibility of ConocoPhillips' dividend growth rate exceeding its current 6% cost of equity into the end of the decade. Margolin expects COP to sustain an 8% dividend compound annual growth rate through 2029 while repurchasing $6.4 billion of stock annually, and reducing net debt-to-capital to less than 9% by the end of 2028 based on the assumption of an average Brent crude oil price of $75 a barrel.

"The message on the 3Q call was similar to that of 2Q, reiterating the moderation in reinvestment rate and commitment to dividend growth through the Willow start-up and after," Margolin said.

Margolin ranks No. 189 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 69% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.6%. See ConocoPhillips Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

#2777. The "carnival" came back to town on Thursday, and Joe Milton III won his "teddy bear"

Fifty-two years after the Thanksgiving legend of Clint Longley was born, a guy who couldn’t convince the Cowboys to give him a spot on the 53-man roster pulled a Reverse Longley on America’s Team.

On Thursday, former Cowboys practice-squad quarterback Joe Milton III returned to Dallas and made them regret not putting him on the roster when they had the chance.

The Cowboys acquired Milton in April 2025, sending a fifth-round pick to New England for Milton and a seventh-round pick. He appeared in four games last season before losing the training-camp and preseason competition with Sam Howell to be the backup to Dak Prescott.

Milton was released in late August. He cleared waivers. He joined the Dallas practice squad. After Eagles quarterback Jalen Hurts landed on injured reserve due to blood clots, the Eagles came calling with a spot on the active roster. The Cowboys could have attempted to add Milton to their own active roster, but they didn’t.

Milton’s impressive performance has rekindled the comments made by Cowboys coach Brian Schottenheimer in the days before the 2026 rosters were cut from 90 to 53.

“I first remember hearing about Joe Milton he was at [the] University of Michigan, and everyone applauded him for how hard and how far he could throw the ball,” Schottenheimer said at the time, via Jane Slater of NFL Network. “Cool. That’s awesome. You know, go be in a carnival somewhere.  Like, that’s not what playing NFL quarterback is. It’s not, ‘Yeah, I can throw the ball 85 yards.’ You’re throwing through small windows, you’re throwing over people, you’re changing arm slots.  So, like, to me, arm strength is cool but, like, you know, go to the fair and win a teddy bear.”

In fairness to Schottenheimer, he then praised Milton’s development.

“But you got to play quarterback, you know, and that’s what I think you see Joe learning is he’s playing quarterback,” Schottenheimer said at the time. “And he’s playing quarterback at a high level.  He’s checking the ball down. He’s making blitz adjustments. He’s solving problems. He’s reading, you know, from his first, second, to third and even fourth progression at times. And it takes time. I mean, it, you know, you don’t stop. I mean, you don’t stop learning as a quarterback and the only way to do it is to play and I’m really, really proud of him.”

Still, the Cowboys weren’t sufficiently proud of Milton to give him a roster spot. And owner/G.M. Jerry Jones refused to admit that they made a mistake.

“That is the best I’ve seen him play,” Jones told reporters after the 39-38 loss. “And he picked a bad time to do it, from my standpoint.”

From Milton’s standpoint, he picked the ideal time to do it. He’ll now become a factor in the Eagles’ future plans at quarterback. How could he not be after what he accomplished on Thursday, engineering a comeback in a game that saw the Eagles down by 21 points late in the third quarter?

Still, it’s just getting started for Milton. He needs to build on what he did. If he does, he’ll become a long-time NFL starter. If he doesn’t, he’ll become another Clint Longley — whose Thanksgiving Day performance in 1974 wasn’t the beginning but the end.

#2776. The Hawks’ second unit is giving the team life

The Atlanta Hawks are one of the top teams in the Eastern Conference through 17 games, and that might be a surprise when looking at some of the circumstances they've played under so far. Jalen Johnson's shooting numbers have taken a dip, CJ McCollum is sidelined with a hamstring strain, Asa Newell could miss most of the season, Jock Landale has a right great toe sprain, and Mouhamed Gueye has yet to step on the floor.

Most of these players have a huge impact on the team in some way or another, yet the Hawks still rise. Some would think it's because someone has stepped up in the starting lineup, and while that is true (Dyson Daniels is playing his best basketball), there's an even bigger reason for the team's success.

It's the second unit, and it's been the collection of several players that has helped the Hawks through this stretch.

If you need bold proof, look no further than their game against the Detroit Pistons, where the lineup of Daniels-Corey Kispert-Aaron Wiggins-Lu Dort-Zuby Ejiofor went on an 18-0 run to start the fourth quarter, and ultimately helped the Hawks win the game.

After that game, head coach Quin Snyder gave credit to that group for how they played.

"That five, just the level of tenacity, defensively was unique. And you could feel it. The way they competed on the offensive boards. They took 3s; they crashed and defended. That's what we were trying to accomplish, and we came out and did it," Snyder said.

It just hasn't been that game, but during the team's current winning streak, they've shown up at key parts of the game. The one player who has bought into his role and is playing well in that second unit is Corey Kispert, who finished with 18 points in 18 minutes against the Pistons. Outside of that game, Kispert is shooting the ball at a high level this season (48.8% from 3), and his defense has also improved.

The crazy thing is that Kispert wasn't in the main rotation to start the season, but with the several injuries across the board, he's had to play minutes. Those minutes have been positive for the Hawks, whatever they look like, and he's contributing to winning.

Ejiofor is another player who's seen minutes due to injuries, and with Landale still sidelined, he's making the most of his time on the court. His rebounding has been key for the second unit, and his defense in general is an upgrade from Landale's. He's shown that he isn't afraid to shoot 3-pointers, but he's making his presence known in the paint more than anything.

Dort's impact off the bench hasn't been as big as many would've expected, and that's mostly because he's playing sort of out of position. With most of the forward depth being hurt, Dort is playing more at the 4, and though he can defend almost any position on the floor, his rebounding isn't the best.

In the game against the Pistons, he was used perfectly, with Dort defending on the perimeter and Daniels playing at the 4, which is good for the team's rebounding numbers. Dort still has a huge impact on defense, and it shows in moments like this.

Though Wiggins was in the starting lineup for some of the games during the current winning streak, it still can't be overlooked what he's done as a whole this season coming off the bench. His scoring has been key for the Hawks in the second unit, and whether that's knowing down 3-pointers or getting to the paint, it's obvious that he's being aggressive when it comes to putting the ball in the basket.

It's no surprise that Wiggins is in early conversations for Sixth Man of the Year, and it would be well deserved if he continues his strong play.

With the way this collection of guys is playing, the Hawks may have some decisions to make when players like McCollum and Landale return from injury. What happens to guys like Kispert and Ejiofor, who are currently playing well? What happens when Gueye, who is one of the Hawks' better defenders, returns from injury?

The Hawks will answer those questions when the time comes, but as of now, it sounds like a good problem to have.

#2775. Tony Gonzalez calls Aaron Rodgers 'selfish' after Steelers flame-out

The Pittsburgh Steelers face the Denver Broncos on Black Friday in their first game since their stunning, midseason breakup with Aaron Rodgers.

The Steelers released Rodgers after the 42-year-old quarterback went scorched-earth against his now-former coach, Mike McCarthy, in an appearance on "The Pat McAfee Show." Rodgers accused McCarthy of sleeping in team meetings and breaking down a Steelers huddle with a "1, 2, 3, Packers!" chant among a litany of grievances following his benching in favor of Mason Rudolph.

Rodgers publicly blasting McCarthy has been met with the condemnation of many former NFL players, so it was little surprise to see Amazon Prime's studio panel rail against the quarterback's comments ahead of the Steelers vs. Broncos game.

Tony Gonzalez was particularly perturbed by Rodgers' actions, referring to them as "selfish" and the entire situation as "sad."

"It's really sad for everybody," Gonzalez said. "For me, for Aaron Rodgers to do what he did, it's a selfish thing to do. It makes no sense. You're talking (out) two sides of your mouth when you say it's insane the way Mike McCarthy's acting, but you're the one who vouched for him to come over here and get him in the building."

"Then, you nuke everybody," he added. "You nuke your teammates, a playoff football team. I just think it makes no sense."

Gonzalez, who played 17 NFL seasons before transitioning to broadcasting in 2014, also acknowledged he could not recall a blowup as explosive as Rodgers' across any sport.

"It's one of those things that's gonna go down in history. I've never seen anything like this in my life," Gonzalez opined. "And we've played this game for a long time, and not just in football, basketball, hockey, it is unprecedented in what went on."

"It's like a little hint of narcissism on his point, to come out on air on 'The Pat McAfee Show' and do what he did," he added. "And there's no excuse for it."

Meanwhile, Ryan Fitzpatrick referred to Rodgers as both "one of the greatest quarterbacks of all time" but also noted the 42-year-old is "one of the most complicated athletes of all time."

What did Fitzpatrick mean by that? He outlined that Rodgers has almost universally been loved and appreciated by his teammates; but at what may be his final stop, he left them out to dry following his comments on "The Pat McAfee Show" before also effectively trying to dispose of McCarthy to save himself.

"The complicated part is, what did he do? He blew everything up. He sees the locker room like a chessboard is what I feel like. All the pieces he can manipulate and move – they're very precious to him as long as they're protecting the king. But as soon as they don't have value and they can't protect the king, then all of sudden they become expendable. He throws them out, and that's what happened with Mike McCarthy."

"You look back to Week 1 – the moment they had – when he gave him the game ball and how much raw emotion there was and how much it meant to him and how much he knew it meant to Mike," Fitzpatrick added. "Fast forward three months later and we have what we have. He sacrificed him as a second thought in his career and I don't think it was the right thing to do."

Richard Sherman was in agreement with both Fitzpatrick and Gonzalez about Rodgers making the wrong choice about the manner in which to air his grievances.

However, Sherman also acknowledged Rodgers' criticism of McCarthy as being warranted, and the former Seattle Seahawks star openly wondered how that might impact the 63-year-old's job security moving forward.

"I don't think it was the right thing to do at all," Sherman said, referencing Rodgers' comments. "I also know that a child that isn't embraced by the village will burn the village down to feel its warmth. And I think that's what Aaron Rodgers did."

"He felt betrayed by the front office, the coaching staff and Mike McCarthy," Sherman explained. "But I think even if you say, 'Hey, this is an indictment of Aaron Rodgers,' (it's) terrible what he did, I agree. It is a sacred place – the locker room. But that's true. The things he said are true. It is also an indictment on Mike McCarthy and the culture that he's built."

"You talk about the Pittsburgh Steelers and what that logo stands for," he continued. "It stands for stability. It's regal. It's respected in the National Football League and you never think of anything like this happening. Dysfunction. What did Tomlin say? It's a dumpster fire? You've never heard those things associated with the Pittsburgh Steelers. Their head coaches coach for 15, 20 years – each one of them. I don't see how Mike McCarthy follows in those footsteps after what's happened."

McCarthy will get a chance to state his case to remain the Steelers' leader as the 2026 NFL season continues. His first opportunity to right the ship will come on Black Friday against the Broncos.

As for Rodgers, he could consider looking for another opportunity after his flame-out, but more likely, the soon-to-be 43-year-old will ride into the sunset to close a 22-year NFL career.

#2774. 'Korean Zombie' pens heartfelt tribute after coach Eddie Cha's death

Coach Eddie Cha impacted the lives of many notable fighters – as was evident by the swath of tributes that flooded social media after his death Monday night.

The MMA history books will likely write Cha's legacy as having been most notably associated with two fighters: one being former double champion Henry Cejudo, the other, "The Korean Zombie" Chan Sung Jung.

On Wednesday, Jung published a heartfelt statement on social media, clearly still processing the news.

"Being here at your home, Coach, it finally feels real," Jung wrote. "I see myself in all the memorial messages others have left, too. I loved you so much, Coach, truly. I miss you so terribly. I just can't stop the tears. You lived such a good life, and you were such a wonderful person. I hope you find your way to a good, peaceful place. We will meet again, I know it – and I'll be waiting for that day. I love you so very much, my friend."

The lead striking coach at Fight Ready MMA in Glendale, Ariz., Cha served as lead corner for Jung in the later parts of his career, through his retirement. When Jung placed his gloves down center-canvas in an emotional August 2023 retirement, Cha was the first team member he embraced. The two shared a lengthy hug as the crowd sang "Zombie" by the Cranberries.

Cha's cause of death has not been publicized. He was 53 years old. A GoFundMe has been set up to support funeral costs and his family going forward.

#2773. Bills benefited from extra seconds during Chiefs' final drive

We saw it on Monday night, when the clock operator didn’t press the button to save a second or two for the Bengals to try to pull out a win over the Commanders.

It apparently happened again on Thursday night, in more subtle fashion.

By our count, the Buffalo clock operator managed to preserve four seconds during what was the Chiefs’ final drive of the game.

I’ve watched it, several times. The Chiefs had first and 10 on their own 11. Quarterback Patrick Mahomes dropped the shotgun snap. By the time he recovered the ball, was touched, the whistle blew, and the Bills called a timeout (assuming they did it instantly), four seconds had elapsed. The clock, however, ultimately moved by only one second, from 26 to 25 — not 22.

Then, after running back Emmett Johnson gained five yards on second and 15, 19 seconds remained when the whistle blew. The clock in the NBC score bug at one point shifted from 19 seconds to 20, presumably to match the official time on the stadium scoreboard.

After the third-down incompletion, the clock showed 15 seconds. It should have been 11 seconds.

Following the punt, which was partially blocked, and the return by Greg Dortch, there were four seconds to play. Four seconds. The same four seconds that apparently were preserved through creative clock operation.

Of course, if the clock had shown 11 seconds and not 15 before the punt, the Bills may have not tried to block it. They may have opted to attempt a fair catch followed by a free kick.

Regardless, there was more time on the clock than there should have been. And those extra seconds ensured that the Bills would have just enough time for Tyler Bass to make a walk-off 57-yard field goal.

With so many games decided by such tight margins, there needs to be a better effort to ensure complete accuracy with the game timing, especially as the final seconds of a game tick, tick, tick away.

So when it was time to divvy up the postgame turkey legs, here’s hoping the Bills saved a drumstick for the timekeeper.

#2770. Bills’ Ed Oliver reveals NSFW back and forth with Chiefs player over controversial Rashee Rice play

Bills' Ed Oliver reveals NSFW back and forth with Chiefs player over controversial Rashee Rice play appeared first on ClutchPoints. Add ClutchPoints as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

The Buffalo Bills came away with a narrow Thanksgiving victory over the Kansas City Chiefs, but one controversial replay decision became a major talking point after the game. The call involved a potential late third-down catch by Chiefs wide receiver Rashee Rice, which was overturned and helped shift momentum in Buffalo's favor.

According to ESPN, Bills defensive tackle Ed Oliver revealed an NSFW exchange he had with Chiefs center Creed Humphrey while the officials reviewed the play. Humphrey initially celebrated what he believed was a completed catch, telling Oliver, "Yeah, they caught it." However, after watching the replay, Oliver disagreed. "Aw s—, he dropped it," the Bills defender recalled saying. Humphrey responded with disbelief, but Oliver remained convinced the ball had hit the ground. He added that the overturned call gave Buffalo's defense a much-needed "boost of energy" to get off the field.

The disputed play came with less than two minutes remaining in a 10-10 tie. Patrick Mahomes connected with Rice for what initially appeared to be an 11-yard reception on third-and-10. After reviewing the footage, NFL officials ruled the pass incomplete, explaining that Rice lost control when his hand slid up the ball as he hit the ground.

#2769. Zach Ertz: People don't understand how good DeVonta Smith is

Eagles wide receiver DeVonta Smith missed five games with a hamstring injury earlier this year and the Eagles lost all five of them, which doesn’t feel like much of a coincidence after watching him in Dallas on Thursday.

Smith caught 11 passes for 200 yards and two touchdowns in Philadelphia’s remarkable 39-38 comeback win. The touchdowns came on 50- and 45-yard strikes from quarterback Joe Milton and Smith shook off a hard landing on his tailbone in the fourth quarter to finish out the game.

The 11 catches tied Smith’s career best and the 200 yards were a personal record, but the big day didn’t come as a shock to tight end Zach Ertz.

“It’s not surprising, honestly,” Ertz said, via Dave Zangaro of NBCSportsPhiladelphia.com. “I watched him when he was at Alabama, winning the Heisman. Comes in here right away, his game is just so smooth. I don’t think people understand how good of a player he truly is until he gets his opportunities. I don’t know what the final stats were. I know over 200 yards, which is crazy. But he’s just a receiver that really has no weakness. The way he runs routes is so smooth, the way he can make plays on the balls in the air, yards after catch. I think he’s probably one of the most underrated players in the league. I saw him as a rookie so where he’s at now doesn’t surprise me at all.”

The Eagles made Smith their No. 1 wideout when they traded A.J. Brown this offseason and the injury interrupted his bid to prove he’s the right guy to fill that role on their offense, but Thanksgiving offered some proof of concept for the Eagles’ passing game.

#2768. The biggest money-making energy trade looks over. What to buy instead

Is one of the greatest money-making trades of the last few years coming to an end?

No, I'm not talking about Nvidia or any of the data center- or AI-related power companies (although they have been crushed lately too… more on that on CNBC TV. Tune in!).

I'm talking about whether the epic boom in refinery-related stocks is unwinding. Over the last year, the jump in some of these companies has been nothing short of remarkable.

That jump is largely due to massive increases in margins, earnings, and cash flow. Those have occurred because of a big jump in what is called a crack spread. That is the difference between the price of oil and the finished price of refined products such as diesel and jet fuel. For most of this year, spreads have been soaring as disruptions and attacks on facilities and shipping in the Arabian Gulf, Hormuz, and deep inside Russia have played havoc with the market. In the last week or so, however, something big has changed: many of those spreads have started to tighten.

This seismic drop in spreads is causing a selloff in the aforementioned refinery companies.

If spreads continue to fall, these stocks may well keep selling off from recent highs. The upshot for investors may be that, as the shares come down, analysts see more upside. The median price target for Phillips 66 (PSX) now indicates a 20% gain from here, while smaller-cap Par Pacific (PARR) is double that.

Here are a few other key points to focus on heading into the Thanksgiving-shortened week.

I like to keep politics as much at arm's length as possible — we all know that energy and politics (sadly) often go hand in hand. Energy policies coming from Washington, D.C., can drive — or destroy — investment dollars. With all the big shifts in the White House over the last 10 years (four years of Trump, four years of Biden, and now 2 years into Trump #2), energy policies can blow through Washington faster than a summer squall hits a wind turbine. But because federal policies can mean so much for specific companies, sectors, and investments, it's important to follow the news flow.

That said, I'd like to take a moment to introduce you to the Bipartisan American Affordability and Jobs Act, known as BAAJA (and pronounced like the state in Mexico). Like much legislation, BAAJA is an obscure but important bill working its way through the American Congress. BAAJA does a lot of things, but what Power Insider readers care about is its focus on energy permitting reform. In a nutshell, BAAJA would help speed up the building of new electricity infrastructure. Again, that's an overly simplistic view of a big bill, but it needs to be on your radar. The utility trade group Edison Electric Institute came out in favor of the legislation this month after the industry received some 11th-hour changes. See more about it in our recent interview with EEI President Drew Maloney here.

The bill passed the U.S. Senate in a vote late Thursday night. Now it moves to the House. Support for its final passage is also coming from the big LNG trade group, whose executive director said he "applauds the Senate for its bipartisan effort to pass meaningful, bipartisan, and durable permitting reforms aimed at delivering greater predictability, consistency, and certainty in the permitting, production, and export process for U.S. LNG. This legislation moves us closer to untangling red tape and eliminating overlapping agency reviews that can keep U.S. energy projects delayed for years."

The Natural Gas Supply Association also chimed in, saying it urges Congress to see the bill as "a resource-agnostic and balanced reform agenda for boosting American energy security. It keeps strong environmental reviews in place, but it draws a firm line against the endless procedural delays that tie critical energy projects in knots for a decade."

In my view, passing BAAJA is an important test of Congress's appetite for helping the energy and electrical power industry, lowering costs, and speeding time to market. The old adage was correct: time is money. Literally. If BAAJA moves on through the House, we could see an upside move in some utility stocks and any company related to energy infrastructure.

Speaking of politics, the news is getting lost in the massive Anthropic (ANT) IPO, but the Texas Commission on Environmental Quality (TCEQ) — whose importance I wrote about in the last edition — just decided to resume permitting for data centers. The Texas Tribune did a nice write-up on it, and you can read it here.

It's tough to say, but it's true. The two are inextricably linked by policies created at both the federal and state levels. As I wrote in the last edition, some federally elected officials may have less impact on state-level issues — like the data center buildout — but they certainly have power over national laws. While many of you are enthralled, disgusted, or confused by sordid tales of Nvidia and Jensen-gate, keep one eye open on what happens in Washington, D.C.

Have a great week, and have a wonderful Thanksgiving, everyone.

Perhaps the big investor story — which is, like most things lately, related to Jensen-gate and Nvidia—is the sizable selloff in many AI, neocloud, and data center power-related stocks.

Painful for current shareholders, but big drops can also bring big opportunities. Wall Street believes that's the case, at least right now. Look at some of the potential upside based on the stocks' prices versus their price targets.

We spoke with the CEO of Applied Digital (APLD) about all of this on CNBC last week, and you can watch it here. He highlighted how the market may be getting these stock stories wrong because, at least with his company, he has long-term contracts with solid-credit companies. In that way, Applied Digital is more like a landlord than a "pure-play" AI company.

My take → While I'm not going to recommend a specific stock, I would add that it is critical for investors to understand the differences between these companies. While they may have some things in common, they are also different, with different business models, customers, leases, and sources of revenue.

Speaking of recommendations… here are two ideas for you:

A recent decline presents a nice opportunity in SOLV Energy (MWH). UBS analyst Jon Windham upgrades SOLV to buy from neutral, believing the market is "underappreciating several key tailwinds for SOLV given uncertainty around data center demand and higher interest rates." UBS highlights how the stock has fallen lately despite making two deals and beating and raising recent guidance. UBS says now is an "attractive entry point" for SOLV. (Side note: MWH is a great stock ticker.)

While markets overall fall, BMO remains bullish on Bloom Energy (BE). After speaking with the company, the analyst is upgrading Bloom to outperform, saying he remains "increasingly confident of BE's commercial traction" and that he still has conviction even with the recent drop in shares. While analyst Ameet Thakkar did cut his target to $230 from $260, that's still 50% upside from the current price. Bloom shares have tumbled 45% since October 1.

I was recently asked for my opinion on the price of oil. Here's my take. The market clearly wants to push oil contract prices lower and has for a few months. But, as I've said and reported for a while, don't confuse the futures (paper) price with the actual price. Yes, shipping rates and insurance rates have come down from recent highs — and there is a good amount of oil now flowing through Hormuz — but the cost of a physical barrel of crude is still well above the futures contract. If we get real, longer-lasting peace around Iran, oil prices will fall. There is plenty of oil in the world. That said, if bombs and missiles start firing again, oil prices will firm. I know that's a non-answer answer, but the oil market is truly headline-driven right now. My late good friend Boone Pickens (later in life, he didn't like being called by the "T" in his first name) used to avoid giving a set price target and instead give a directional quote, so I'll do the same: the odds of oil being lower in two months than it is today are 75%.

Last week, I had a one-on-one with Vimal Kapur, chair and CEO of Honeywell International (HON), at an RBC conference in New York City. While the conversation wasn't filmed, I can tell you that Kapur offered investors reassurance that his order book is doing very, very well. This is important because Honeywell is huge in the things that make other things work. Its sensors go into a ton of energy facilities, from refineries to LNG facilities. Honeywell is a leading indicator of spending and buildouts, so if it keeps getting orders, it's a good sign for macro energy.

Bottom line: Watch Honeywell stock as a potential "tell."

This week's Inside Line interview takes a different approach. We hit our friend Craig Johnson up for actionable insights into energy and stocks. Craig is managing director and chief market technician at Piper Sandler. He recently appeared on Power Lunch to talk about markets and stocks. He mentioned being bullish on energy, so we wanted to dig in just a little bit more.

This week, we had the pleasure of chatting with Balaji Krishnamurthy, president of Chevron Australia. We spoke at length about natural gas, the impact of disruptions, and long-term demand from Chevron customers in the Asia-Pacific region.

This is a bit of a local RBI for the New York City area, but one I felt was important to highlight. New York's electric grid operator just came out with a massive warning for everyone in our region: in just a couple of years, there may not be enough electricity supply to power our needs. A new report shows a deficit in seven years, with a nearly 2 GW power hole in a decade. That's a number of big power plants' worth of energy. Get your solar panels on and buy that backup generator! I'll call this one random, but scary.

#2767. Aaron Rodgers clears waivers

It’s official. Aaron Rodgers is a free agent, again.

For the third time since March 2025, Rodgers can sign with any team at any time.

The soon-to-be 43-year-old cleared waivers on Thursday, per multiple reports.

He can sign whenever he chooses, either to a practice squad or a 53-man roster. He can wait until the postseason, if he chooses.

Of course, someone will have to want to sign him. In the 2026 offseason, only the Steelers did. At this point, an injury to a starter on a contending team will be the one thing that could create immediate interest in his services.

As long as that team is willing to risk Rodgers eventually dialing up McAfee for another elongated monologue of grievances. At this point, any other outcome would be a surprise.

#2766. Fantasy Basketball Weekend Streamers: Matisse Thybulle locking up on defense for Lakers

Matisse Thybulle has a history of being an impactful defender, though injuries have kept him off the court a lot in recent years. While the defensive stats have still been there this season, it's his improvement on the offensive end that has been so impressive and a big reason why he should be rostered in fantasy basketball, even beyond this weekend.

He is averaging 2.3 steals, 1.0 block and 1.8 triples per game this season, and he has scored 14 points in back-to-back games. Thybulle has never been a high-volume scorer, but he hasn't been a liability on that end of the floor this year, which has allowed him to be on the floor to make more of an impact on the defensive end. He has been vital to the Lakers' success as a starter all season in what has been the best year of his career so far.

The Hornets, Bulls, Cavaliers, Mavericks, Rockets, Thunder, Kings and Wizards only play one game this weekend, so avoid those eight teams if you're looking to stream.

With Nolan Traoré set to miss 8-10 weeks with a hamstring injury, Brown has taken over as the starting point guard in Brooklyn. His last game was rough, but he put together a strong performance in his first start with 17 points, seven assists and four three-pointers. It is worth noting that Brown hasn't played both legs of a back-to-back this season due to a back injury, though that doesn't guarantee that trend will continue. If it does, Egor Dёmin will likely start against the Hornets on Saturday since Brown isn't on the injury report ahead of Friday's game against the Raptors.

Sorry, you're going to have to keep seeing Flemings on this list until he's rostered in more leagues. He's shown so many exciting traits as a rookie, and while the box score numbers haven't been great yet, they're coming. He's locked in as a starter, and his role should only continue to increase. He has shown playmaking prowess, and he's an impactful defender, so the steals should come. Once again, Flemings is a good bet for this weekend but should be considered a rest-of-season play as well.

I get it. Melton has had a ton of opportunities to step up with Stephen Curry sidelined, and he just hasn't. That is, until their last game, when he had 24 points, seven assists and eight three-pointers. Melton has a history of fantasy success, and a rough shooting stretch isn't going to scare me away. Hopefully the field goal percentage will be better than it has been, which isn't asking for much, but the assists, steals and three-pointers should be there.

Joe started in Detroit's last game with Cade Cunningham out. Cunningham is currently questionable to play on Friday, and if he remains out, Joe would likely get another start, though Duncan Robinson's continued absence should open up minutes for Joe regardless. There's also a chance that Joe starts if Cunningham is back, with Kevin Huerter heading to the bench. Regardless, Joe should play enough minutes to be worth streaming.

OG Anunoby is doubtful to play on Friday, which means Shamet will get at least one more start. As a starter over New York's last three games, Shamet has averaged 16 points and four three-pointers per game. He hasn't provided much value in other categories, but the points and three-pointers should be there, even when he eventually moves back to a reserve role.

While the poster dunk on Victor Wembanyama was a ton of fun, Cissoko has been able to do way more than that for fantasy managers lately.

He has started the last nine games for Portland and has averaged 10.6 points, 3.7 rebounds, 1.0 steal and 2.2 triples per game during this stretch. It appears he has locked in a starting role until Ja Morant returns, which shouldn't be this weekend. Cissoko has had at least one steal in five straight games, and while he isn't a high-volume scorer, he should be a decent source of triples and swipes.

Graves has started the last two games for Toronto, with the first coming with Collin Murray-Boyles sidelined due to a concussion. However, Graves remained a starter in their last game, even with CMB back. Graves has been solid in that role, so there is certainly a chance he keeps it this weekend. Even if he doesn't, Graves's game is fantasy friendly, as he showcased in his first start, when he had 20 points, 10 rebounds, three steals, one block and two three-pointers. That makes him worth taking a chance on.

His last game was rough, but Wolf has largely been productive as a starter for Brooklyn, which is a role he seems to have locked up. In seven starts this season, Wolf is averaging 11.6 points, 5.7 rebounds, 2.1 assists and 2.3 three-pointers per game. Wolf wasn't in the rotation to start the year, but injuries opened the door for him, and he took advantage. His early-season development, along with Nolan Traore's emergence, gives the Nets some extra hope for their 2025 draft class.

Bassey has started the last four games for the Warriors at center and averaged 12.3 points, 9.3 rebounds, 1.3 steals and 1.3 blocks per game during this stretch. Steve Kerr has said he hopes Bassey remains a starter for the rest of the season, and while Kerr has a history of changing up his starting unit frequently, managers are hoping for some consistency here. For now, he should remain a starter through the weekend.

#2765. Patriots rooting guide for Thanksgiving weekend

Happy Thanksgiving weekend, everybody. While the rest of us are going to enjoy some days with family and food, the NFL is quite busy: a total of 16 games will be played between Wednesday and Monday, with at least one game on on five of the next six days.

One of those will be played by the New England Patriots, who are going to take on the Los Angeles Chargers on Sunday at 4:05 p.m. ET. There are no questions about who to root for in this specific game, but how about the others? Let's take a look.

Once again, a quick explainer: as long as the Patriots are still theoretically alive in the race for a playoff spot, we will root with that possibility in mind.

Denver Broncos (8-2) at Pittsburgh Steelers (6-4): Go Broncos! Now this is a game that truly matters. The Steelers are the current seventh seed in the AFC and as such the Patriots' closest rival in the race for a postseason spot. In fact, if they lose to the Broncos and New England wins in L.A. then the Patriots are suddenly ahead of them in the standings. Luckily for the Patriots, the Steelers are doing their best these days to at least play their part in that. | Prime Video

New Orleans Saints (3-7) at Cincinnati Bengals (7-3): Go Saints! Somebody has to win the AFC North, and the Bengals are the best bet right now. That said, it would be good if they had to fight for playoff positioning until the very last week of the season, just in case the Patriots need them to beat the Browns in Week 18. With. that in mind, we are rooting for the Saints in this one. | CBS

Baltimore Ravens (4-7) at Houston Texans (4-6): Go Ravens! Another somewhat important game from a Patriots point of view. Neither team is currently in the playoff picture, but the Texans have seemingly started hitting their stride and a loss to the moribund Ravens would help break their momentum and make a late push harder. | CBS

New York Giants (6-3-1) at Indianapolis Colts (3-8): Go Colts! Indianapolis entering the playoff race seems like a long shot, meaning that this can be a game where we root with draft position in mind. Don't forget: the Jets earn the Colts' first-rounder next year. | FOX

Tennessee Titans (1-9) at Jacksonville Jaguars (7-3): Go Titans! With games against the Steelers and Texans still on their schedule, the Patriots will want the Jaguars to have to bring their A-game in both contests. Maybe some heat applied from a loss to their division rivals can help with that. | FOX

New York Jets (3-8) at Miami Dolphins (1-9): Go Dolphins! We are rooting for the worse-positioned AFC East team in this one, once again from a draft perspective. | CBS

Atlanta Falcons (3-7) at Minnesota Vikings (7-3): Go Vikings! Four words: strength of schedule tiebreaker. Will it matter? Unlikely. Is it better to be safe than sorry, though? Absolutely. | FOX

Washington Commanders (4-5-1) at Arizona Cardinals (3-8): Go Commanders! We would rather see top draft picks end up with an NFC team like the Cardinals over, for example, the Dolphins or Jets. Sorry, Arizona, it's nothing personal. | FOX

Seattle Seahawks (6-4) at San Francisco 49ers (9-1): Go Seahawks! Once again, strength of schedule makes the difference here. | FOX

Las Vegas Raiders (4-7) at Cleveland Browns (6-4): Go Raiders! A big one for the Patriots on Sunday night. New England has a clear edge over Las Vegas, but is looking up in the standings to the Browns. A Raiders win would therefore be important for two reasons: it would drop Cleveland to 6-5 and also give them a negative result in a common game with the Patriots. That tiebreaker might just end up being important with no head-to-head between the two teams. | NBC

Carolina Panthers (5-5) at Tampa Bay Buccaneers (3-7): Go good game! An all-NFC South battle will close out Thanksgiving weekend on Monday night. It's a low-stress affair from a Patriots perspective. | ESPN

The following games have already been played.

Green Bay Packers (6-5) at Los Angeles Rams (7-4): Go Packers! | GB 13 : 26 LA

Chicago Bears (9-2) at Detroit Lions (8-3): Go good game! | CHI 39 : 35 DET

Philadelphia Eagles (4-7) at Dallas Cowboys (5-7): Go good game! | PHI 39 : 38 DAL

Kansas City Chiefs (8-2) at Buffalo Bills (9-1): Go… Bills? | KC 10 : 13 BUF

#2763. Jerry Jones on Mike McCarthy: "He was outstanding as the head coach"

The recent remarks from former Steelers quarterback Aaron Rodgers regarding Steelers coach Mike McCarthy have raised plenty of questions, posed to plenty of people.

After Thursday’s 39-38 loss to the Eagles, Cowboys owner and G.M. Jerry Jones was asked about McCarthy, who coached Jones’s team for five seasons, from 2020 through 2024.

“I offered him to stay at least another year when he left,” Jones told reporters. “And so I thought he was outstanding as the head coach and had a great command of, frankly, quarterback, offense. Can’t say enough good things about him. He’s, frankly, one of the finest people that around football that I’ve been around. And I can’t say enough about him. I never had the type of thing that is being said about him. I never had that in consideration in any of my time with him, extending his contract. Any of that, that was never on the table. And we never had an issue within the organization.”

That conflicts, obviously, with the things Rodgers said on Wednesday.

Regardless, Jones no longer employs McCarthy. While Jones possibly chose to take the high road, a former employer vouching for a coach is fundamentally different than his current employer vouching for him.

McCarthy took the Cowboys to the playoffs three times in five years, advancing to the divisional round in 2022. The Cowboys failed to qualify for the postseason in the first year after McCarthy left. Thursday’s loss to the Eagles makes it harder for them to get there the second post-McCarthy season.

#2762. Pirates reportedly trade former No. 1 pick Henry Davis to Arizona for utility player Tim Tawa

PITTSBURGH — The Pittsburgh Pirates are sending catcher Henry Davis to Arizona in exchange for utility player Tim Tawa, a person with knowledge of the trade told The Associated Press on Friday.

The person spoke on condition of anonymity because the deal was not yet official.

The Pirates selected Davis with the first overall pick in the 2021 amateur draft, with general manager Ben Cherington hoping he would serve as a foundational piece during a franchise-wide overhaul.

While Davis, who made his MLB debut in 2023, eventually blossomed into one of the better defensive catchers in baseball, he never found solid footing as a hitter.

The 27-year-old Davis has a career batting average of just .178 with 25 homers and 89 RBIs in 283 games, including just a .172 mark last season. Davis excelled defensively and served as Pirates ace Paul Skenes' personal catcher during the pitcher's Cy Young season in 2025.

Davis' departure leaves the Pirates with two catchers on the 40-man roster with MLB experience in Rafael Flores and Endy Rodriguez, though Rodriguez is likely out until late spring 2027 while recovering from surgery to repair a torn labrum in his left hip.

Tawa, 27, hit .240 with 11 homers and 42 RBIs in 104 games last season with the Diamondbacks, bouncing between the outfield and the infield regularly.

#2761. Could there be an award for Al Horford?

The number 40 in the NBA almost always refers to points scored and never a player's age. Al Horford was born about three months before the Nintendo Entertainment System (NES) was sold nationwide in the United States. His birthplace being the Dominican Republic makes this an imperfect example, but Dude Is Old. And 40 is rarity. But how rare?

Not only is he just the 13th player to play a season at 40, he's 1 of just 5 centers age 40+ to get at least 2 starts in a season .

That is certainly some impressive company. I know that Horford is typically compared against LeBron these days because of their forties status (careful: it's a Facebook link!), but let's account for Horford's shorter height relative to most centers to rope in some forwards:

But, to be clear, to go with the first, better list, Al Horford is 1 of 5 centers in NBA history (post-merger) to start at least 2 games in a season at the age of 40 or older. Or, 1 of only 10 forward-center players 40+ to start 2+ games in a season. Is it an all-time "No way!" stat? No, but it's a really surprising result.

We're nearing the quarter mark of the season, so, he's unlikely to get that games started number up much more at this pace, especially as the Dubs get healthy and make a trade for a center. However, if he does get to, say, 8 starts, that's a really small group of players he'll share a stat with, and a notable one.

As Dalton Johnson has been reporting for NBC Sports Bay Area, not only is he now the oldest to ever play for Golden State, he's shocked the league with his 20-year career.

Mike Budenholzer coached Horford his final three seasons with the Atlanta Hawks from 2013 to 2016. Those were Horford's seventh, eighth and ninth NBA seasons. The thought of Horford ever reaching 20 seasons is instantly met with a "no" from Budenholzer.

[…] "In some ways I can because he takes such great care of himself," Budenholzer says. "He is so meticulous with his body and his nutrition and diet and such a pro. […] I'm just happy for him. He's just one of the all-time great guys."

Credit to Horford for still having that fire and putting in the work. The aging curve takes pro athletes from attaining peak performance with ease to a struggle to maintain 80% of that peak. Of course, it's not just keeping the body firm. He crested the wave. In the same Johnson report, he quotes Harford's adaptation to the evolving game: picking up a three-point shot. It was great fun seeing him block and shot and hit a three against Boston the other night.

So… when this season ends, the Warriors should consider some kind of award for Al Horford. No, I did not come up with the title of this post first and work backwards. When he had his first start back on the 11th, I thought about how unusual it was to see a 40-year old in a starting NBA lineup.

Of course, he already has several awards. A 5-time All-Star, an All-NBA team, an All-Rookie team, and All-Defensive team… and, of course, a potential Hall of Fame induction. But the Warriors signed him to a 2-year deal specifically because of what his age brings to the table. There's a perseverance and a dignity to his contributions. The team doesn't have to do this, but maybe The Horford is something the various outlets that cover the team could consider. After review, this could just wind up being the Best Old Guy Award, but there is something to be said about a Best Veteran type of accolade.

#2760. Nvidia surge: What investors should do with the stock and the AI trade now

Nvidia shares gapped higher at the open by more than 30%, on pace for its biggest one-day gain in 23 years on Monday, following the news just before the bell that former Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang is going to be reinstated.

Investors and traders said that while this reflex rally may have more to run, cautiousness is warranted on Nvidia and the AI trade because of the overhang of investigations into the alleged Chinese espionage involving the company and others, as well as the possibility for data center moratoriums as Democrats take control of Congress and key governor posts in January.

"Am I as bullish on Nvidia now as I was on Friday? Of course not, you can't be," said Dan Niles, founder of Niles Investment Management, which counted Nvidia as its largest position as he bought on the dip over the last few weeks. "I'm trimming back."

"All the same issues that were here before Jensen resigned are still here," he added.

The day before Huang resigned on Nov. 12 as CEO, Nvidia closed at $173.73. It's intraday high on Monday was $171.50, up from its recent low around $125 as it was selling off in the wake controversies. The VanEck Semiconductor ETF closed at $506.96 the day before his resignation. It reached a high of $469.35 before losing steam as trading wore on Monday.

Mike Khouw, YieldMax strategist at Tidal Financial Group, told CNBC on Monday that there were "sighs of relief," "applause" and even "cheering" among investors at the news of Huang's potential reinstatement. But he also said there are still considerable risks facing the AI trade.

Jensen Huang was not the only problem at Nvidia, he said, and there are still many vulnerabilities in the overall AI trade, which should give investors pause. Credit-sensitive companies are still at risk of faltering, Khouw added.

The national security implications of the scandal that has rocked Nvidia and the global AI trade more broadly aren't going away any time soon even as the Trump administration assured the Nvidia board there were none. Investors on Wall Street are expressing strategic concerns about ways to play the tech space just as lawmakers in Washington are demanding answers.

Democrats are demanding answers on what exactly happened with Nvidia and the Chinese government.

"The reckless decision to bring Jensen Huang back as CEO does not change the fact that Mr. Huang, NVIDIA executives, and the company's board still owe Congress answers about the company's compliance with U.S. export controls," Sen. Elizabeth Warren (D-Mass.) said in a Monday statement. "The Trump Administration also needs to explain to Congress what 'input' was given and by which senior officials in the United States government to support his reinstatement."

House Ways and Means ranking member Richard Neal (D-Mass.) stressed the importance of Congressional oversight in determining the best course of action to move forward. The outcome of any Congressional investigations will likely be crucial for Nvidia's long-term viability as an investment opportunity, especially since the company does so much international business.

"This is an export control issue," Neal told CNBC on Monday. "Congress is not subservient to this president or any other president as it relates to trade and tariff policies. This is a trade related issue."

Many of the same Democrats will call for a slowdown in the data center buildout, a strong issue for the public partly responsible for their Blue Wave earlier this month.

"You're going to have to work your way through what these data center moratoriums all mean," said Niles. "Now I think the broader market keeps going but I am not as convinced on the move in the data-center centric names from here."

To be sure, many Wall Street analysts were rushing to upgrade Nvidia's stock on Monday after Huang's reinstatement. Most restored price targets well above $200 with one as high as $270. But many traders think the rebound may fail before getting back to those levels.

Famed short seller Michael Burry, who made a boatload betting against Nvidia with put options as it cratered, told his Substack audience that he thinks the rebound here could be "outsized," especially with low volume in the market overall because of the Thanksgiving holiday this week.

But he too sees the rally eventually losing steam.

"Jensen being back without much more information might even restore NVDA to the high 100s," Burry wrote. "I won't short it on this information I have today until it gets closer to or maybe hurdles $200."

#2759. NBC removed polarizing Deshaun Watson from promo hyping Browns-Raiders ‘SNF’ clash

On second thought, NBC doesn't really feel the need to use Deshaun Watson for promotional ads for this week's "Sunday Night Football" clash.

After previously using the polarizing Browns quarterback with a checkered past to promote this Sunday's Raiders-Browns game in Cleveland, NBC removed Watson from its video package promoting the game and instead went with linebacker Carson Schwesinger, as noted by Ari Meirov.

He tweeted a collage showing last Sunday's promo featuring Watson alongside Raiders rookie quarterback Fernando Mendoza, compared to Thursday's version absent Watson.

It's certainly a notable omission due to Watson's transgressions.

Watson faced 27 civil lawsuits alleging misconduct, including allegations of him exposing himself, touching them with his privates and kissing them without consent.

The NFL suspended him 11 games in 2022 and fined him $5 million over the allegations ahead of his first season with the Browns.

How the NFL media has covered Watson has since become a hot topic, with some getting called out for glossing over the topic.

Prime Video's Al Michaels drew criticism earlier this year for not discussing the topic during a Steelers-Browns "Thursday Night Football clash."

"If you follow football, you know the story — we don't have to go through the whole Deshaun Watson thing," Michaels said.

Cleveland Browns quarterback Deshaun Watson (4) celebrates after an NFL football game against the Houston Texans in Cleveland, Sunday, Nov. 15, 2026. AP Photo/Sue Ogrocki

While replacing a quarterback for another star player in a promo graphic is not unheard of, it stands out in this game since Schwesinger is not a household name.

The second-year linebacker is having a strong season for the upstart Browns, yet he's not a well-known star like Aaron Donald or Micah Parsons.

NBC is airing this game since the original Sunday night game featuring the Patriots and Chargers holds the entertainment value of watching grass grow.

Houston Texans wide receiver Nico Collins (12) is tackled by Cleveland Browns linebacker Carson Schwesinger (49) on Sunday, Nov. 15, 2026, in Cleveland. IMAGN IMAGES via Reuters Connect

The Patriots' season may be over with Drake Maye possibly sidelined for the year and the Chargers are 1-9 and may be without quarterback Justin Herbert.

The league then instead flexed to this matchup featuring a pleasant surprise in Cleveland, which currently owns a playoff spot, against the 2026 No. 1 pick in Mendoza.

Even if NBC doesn't use Watson in the promotional material, his troubling past is a storyline that Mike Tirico and Cris Collinsworth will decide how to address.

#2758. Jamar Taylor honored as a 2027 Navy All-American

Wide receiver Jamar Taylor (Wilmington, Delaware/ Salesianum School) was officially honored as a 2027 Navy All-American during a virtual jersey presentation as part of the Road to the Dome tour.

Taylor will play in the annual East vs. West matchup inside the Alamodome in San Antonio, Texas at 1 p.m. ET on Saturday, January 9, 2027, live on NBC and Peacock, which marks the twenty-seventh anniversary of the event.

The nationwide Road to the Dome tour will honor 100 Navy All-Americans with live and virtual jersey presentations. The series will be released weekly through social platforms across NBC Sports.

Taylor was selected by the Navy All-American Bowl Selection Committee, comprised of the All-American Bowl, Andrew Ivins (247Sports), Brandon Huffman (Rivals), and NXGN. Navy All-Americans are eligible for the Navy All-American Bowl Player of the Year Award, Anthony Muñoz Lineman of the Year Award, Navy All-American Bowl Defensive Player of the Year Award, Navy All-American Bowl Man of the Year, and Navy All-American Bowl Game MVP Award.

As an NBC Sports-owned property, the All-American Bowl is part of a marquee lineup of elite events that includes the Olympics and Paralympics, the NBA, WNBA, Major League Baseball, Notre Dame Football, the Premier League, and primetime's #1 show for an unprecedented 15 consecutive years: Sunday Night Football. The All-American Bowl is annually the most-watched, most-talked about, and most-prestigious high school all-star event with more than four million unique television viewers and more than 25,000 fans in attendance. The history and tradition of the All-American Bowl is unparalleled, as it features: 665 draft picks; 110 Super Bowl champions; 300 Pro Bowl selections; and 18 Heisman finalists. For more information, visit nbcsports.com/college-football/navy-all-american-bowl or follow us on Facebook, X, and Instagram (@AABonNBC).
